It preserves the difference between mathematical integration of increments and epistemic warrant for their calibration. [S9]

Let $`G=(V,E)`$ be a finite undirected graph. For each edge, consider both orientations and give an integer increment $`d(u,v)`$ satisfying

```math
d(v,u)=-d(u,v).
\quad\text{(5)}\quad
```

Let $`L:V\to\mathbb Z`$ be an independently fixed and warranted truth-loss function. Lower values of $`L`$ are better in the specified respect. Choose a spanning forest $`T`$ of $`G`$. A fundamental cycle is the cycle formed by an edge outside $`T`$ together with the unique tree path joining its endpoints, with a consistent orientation when its increments are summed.

**Proposition 3 (finite calibration criterion).** The following statements are equivalent:

1. On every oriented edge, $`d(u,v)=L(u)-L(v)`$.
2. The same equality holds on the forest edges, and the sum of $`d`$ around every fundamental cycle is zero.
3. There is a potential $`N:V\to\mathbb Z`$ with $`d(u,v)=N(v)-N(u)`$ on every edge and, on each connected component $`C`$, a constant $`c_C`$ such that $`N(v)=c_C-L(v)`$.

**Proof.** The first statement implies the second because the loss differences telescope around every closed cycle. It implies the third by choosing any integer $`c_C`$ for each component and defining $`N(v)=c_C-L(v)`$.

Now assume the second statement. On a component choose a root $`r`$ and set $`N(r)=0`$. For any vertex $`v`$, define $`N(v)`$ by summing $`d`$ along the unique forest path from $`r`$ to $`v`$. The forest calibration gives

```math
N(v)=L(r)-L(v).
\quad\text{(6)}\quad
```

For a nonforest edge $`(u,v)`$, take the fundamental cycle consisting of the forest path from $`u`$ to $`v`$ followed by the edge from $`v`$ back to $`u`$. Its zero sum says

```math
N(v)-N(u)+d(v,u)=0.
\quad\text{(7)}\quad
```

Antisymmetry therefore gives $`d(u,v)=N(v)-N(u)=L(u)-L(v)`$. This proves the first statement on all edges. Finally, the third statement immediately yields the first by substitution. $`\square`$

If two potentials have the same edge differences on a connected component, their difference is constant there: traverse a path between any two vertices and telescope. The component constants in the proposition are therefore the whole freedom, not an omitted global calibration parameter.

### 7.1 Consequences and controls

Every sequence of accepted edges with $`d(u,v)>0`$ strictly decreases $`L`$. It cannot form a directed cycle. In a finite graph it cannot revisit a vertex and hence contains at most $`|V|-1`$ edges. With integer loss it also has length at most the difference between its initial loss and the minimum loss in the component. Neither bound establishes that an improving outgoing edge always exists or that a terminal point has globally minimal loss.

Acyclicity alone is weaker than calibration. Any finite directed acyclic graph has an ordinal rank increasing along its arrows, but that does not realize arbitrarily prescribed numerical increments. For example, increments of one on $`a\to b`$ and $`b\to c`$, together with an increment of three on $`a\to c`$, form no directed cycle but cannot be exact differences of a single potential. The two routes from $`a`$ to $`c`$ have different totals.

Even exact differences need not be calibrated to truth. Choosing $`N=L`$ instead of $`N=-L`$ gives an exact potential whose increases worsen loss. A forest can be perfectly calibrated while a newly admitted nonforest edge violates the required cycle equality. A coherent potential unrelated to the true target supplies order without truth orientation. Accidental correct conclusions can occur within such an order without converting the mechanism's function into truth-directed correction.

These controls explain the importance of a complete, fixed edge registry. Adding a new revision edge changes the object being certified. The previous forest-and-cycle checks need not cover it. Likewise, changing the intended truth-loss function changes the calibration claim even when every stored increment remains identical.

The proposition is consequently a finite, exact result conditional on the declared graph and independently warranted $`L`$. It does not discover truth-loss from the increments, certify a metaphysical source, or prove that a real subject's learning is globally sound. Its constructive content is that the whole finite calibration claim can be verified using a forest and the associated fundamental cycles.

### 7.2 From an exact potential to a warranted correction

The calibration theorem establishes a real mathematical achievement. It does not make “correction” one scalar property that silently includes source authenticity, truth, function, authority and a recipient's action. The public treatment of norm provenance distinguishes thirteen questions which can now be joined to the theorem and to the account of wise direction in §6. Their distinctness is semantic, not a claim that they are probabilistically independent or that every assignment of answers is possible. A substantive theory may entail relations between them; those entailments must be stated and defended. [S32]

Fix the standard and its version, the graph and increment units, the function account, the proposed bearer, the episode and time, the recipient and the target proposition or action. These indices matter: a correct result for one recipient, environment or standard does not become a result for another by omitting the argument places.

| Question | What a positive answer must establish |
|---|---|
| Declared increment | What each admitted transition's correction label means and how its value is obtained. |
| Path independence | Exact differences of a potential on the registered graph, under the orientation and cycle conventions of §7. |
| Transition coverage | Why the graph includes the transitions relevant to the claimed scope. |
| Temporal validity | Why the standard and labels persist, or how they validly transport between versions. |
| Standard provenance | Which source, objective or governing norm is actually being represented, at the specified edition and scope. |
| Semantic fidelity | Whether the labels represent the intended correction relation accurately. |
| World connection | Why the represented improvement is truth-conducive or otherwise fitting in the actual domain. |
| Function fixation | Why this mechanism has the claimed function under the specified causal-role, selected-effect, personal-design, learned-objective, proper-function or fiṭrah account. |
| Bearer applicability | Whether the norm and function apply to this bearer and this exercise. |
| Apprehended reason | Whether the bearer actually selects because it apprehends the fitting reason. |
| Authority | Whether the relevant prescriptive or revelational title is genuine and applies here. |
| Recipient warrant | Whether this recipient has the required source-and-content route, uptake and defeat conditions. |
| Execution | Whether the proposed correction is actually performed. |

One can define successive assessment contracts by adjoining these conditions: mathematical exactness; covered and temporally valid operation; accurate representation of the standard; truth-connected operation; account-specific function; bearer-local reason integration; applicable authority; recipient readiness; and executed correction. These conjunctions are useful specifications of a **particular claimed achievement**. They are not universal definitions of normativity or a necessary ladder for every true judgment. A reflex correction can be truth-directed without the reflective reason-uptake demanded by a wise intentional action. A recipient can acquire ordinary knowledge without occupying a revelational authority relation. A truth-directed function can exist even if an investigator has not yet identified it. The conditions for the achievement and the investigator's evidence for them must also remain distinct. [S32; §§5–6]

The mathematical positive result is correspondingly precise. Where the graph is adequately covered for the task, the increments satisfy §7, and an independently warranted stable standard supplies the truth-loss calibration, $`N(v)=c_C-L(v)`$ on each connected component C. Therefore the potential compares two states in the same component by exactly the warranted loss difference, independently of the path used to compare them. Across different components, the constants $`c_C`$ are independent; their relative ranking requires additional calibration. A potential does not acquire that missing information by being plotted on one common axis.

This result joins formal consistency to an independently justified standard. It does not infer the standard from consistency. For example, a score faithfully representing a systematically misleading doctrine can be exact, stable and semantically faithful to that doctrine while failing its world connection. A truth-correlated score can lack the historical or constitutive function relation required by the proposed account. Conversely, a mechanism historically selected or personally designed for truth can fail in an unsuitable current environment. A component may perfectly perform a manipulative causal role. Observed reliability without the rest of a proper-function account is not that account's warrant. These controls preserve actual explanatory differences rather than treating all function vocabulary as equivalent. [S32]

A learned objective needs its own function-fixing account: the relevant training or reward history must be connected to the identity of the objective now being pursued. Present success at that objective does not establish how it was acquired, that its target is true or fitting, or that it carries normative authority. The same empirical performance can therefore leave different historical and normative questions open. [S32]

Other controls concern the stronger personal and practical relations. An impersonal exact optimizer can reach the stipulated correct states without apprehending them as reasons. Several agents can distribute source custody, discrimination, authority and execution without one bearer satisfying the complete local package. Formally, $`\forall r\,\exists b\,P(r,b)`$ does not imply $`\exists b\,\forall r\,P(r,b)`$: two roles assigned exclusively to two different bearers give an immediate counterexample. A genuinely integrated collective may satisfy an appropriate collective account, but that account is an additional premise. Likewise a personal author can select an arbitrary or vicious standard, and a praiseworthy output can arise accidentally. Neither authorship nor outcome quality alone gives the wise direction of §6. [S32]

An abstract-order account with a concrete actualizer makes a further explicit allocation. It can place the standard, its semantic relations and its truth connection in the proposed abstract order, while assigning function, bearer application, uptake, authority relations, recipient warrant and execution to the concrete mechanism or agents. This allocation is a rival explanatory proposal, not a demonstration that an abstract entity speaks or that a mechanism apprehends reasons. The applicability of the abstract standard, ownership of the relevant acts and integration in one bearer remain distinct bridges. Its formal description alone neither proves those bridges nor refutes a stronger source account that supplies them. [S32]

A genuine authority can also be inaccessible to a recipient; a warranted recipient can fail to act. The sourcehood treatment's [account of informed self-governance and authenticated disclosure](received-actuality-and-intrinsic-necessity.md#12-self-knowledge-sovereignty-and-practical-conformance) supplies richer affirmative arguments for some of those relations. The numerical countermodels do not refute premises they omit. Conversely, a theological attribution of Wisdom cannot fill an unmeasured human mechanism or actual communication merely by giving its coordinate a favorable name.

The vector does not prove that information about several coordinates cannot be encoded in one number. A finite tuple can be encoded injectively, and a separately defended theory can define a composite score. The defect is using an uncalibrated number as evidence for meanings it does not establish, or erasing a distinction needed by the intended use. Similarly, observing that a conjunction is false need not identify which conjunct failed. With two requirements, the alternatives (0,1) and (1,0) both make the conjunction false while requiring different repairs. If each required coordinate is independently determined and its substantive bridge holds, their conjunction is determined too. That constructive composition is useful; the encoding itself supplies no missing evidence.

The inherited norm-provenance proposal does not provide fresh execution evidence for its historical checker or a complete formalization of these indices. Its intake correction reports a narrower repaired formal signature and an absent checker source. The proposed Wisdom citation also has a corrected locator: PDF position 227 corresponds to printed page 221, not printed 227. That is a correction reported by the public intake, not a newly inspected translation page here. The source passage is not credited with stating this entire vector, and the six guarded a-fortiori conditions remain the project's synthesis unless a primary source jointly supports them. A complete philosophical or theological application still owes its actual source, world, bearer and recipient premises. [S32]

### 7.3 Transporting a correction scale through time

Per-version exactness alone does not establish that two versions represent the same norm. The missing comparison can be made constructive. Let $`N_0:V_0\to\mathbb R`$ and $`N_1:V_1\to\mathbb R`$ be exact potentials for two graph versions with fixed increment units. Let $`\phi:V_0\to V_1`$ be a justified bijective correspondence of the states being compared. Choose a spanning forest H on $`V_0`$ whose edges occur in the old graph and whose corresponding edges occur in the new graph. Suppose their increments agree:

```math
d_1(\phi(u),\phi(v))=d_0(u,v)
\qquad ((u,v)\text{ an oriented edge of }H).
```

**Proposition — Exact temporal transport.** On every connected component C of H there is a constant $`k_C`$ such that

```math
N_1(\phi(v))=N_0(v)+k_C\qquad(v\in C).
```

Consequently all potential differences, and hence all rankings, within C are preserved. One matched value in C fixes its additive constant.

**Proof.** Put $`F(v)=N_1(\phi(v))-N_0(v)`$. Exactness and increment agreement give $`F(v)-F(u)=0`$ on each forest edge. Every two vertices of C are joined by a forest path; telescoping gives equality of F along that path. Its common value is $`k_C`$. Subtracting two displayed equations preserves their difference, and one matched value determines the constant. ∎

This is an elementary composition of the exact-potential argument with a warranted state correspondence. If H connects the whole comparison domain, every pairwise ranking there is preserved. If H is disconnected, only within-component comparisons follow. For example, old and new exact potentials on three states can be $`(0,1,2)`$ and $`(0,1,-1)`$. Their increment on the common edge from the first to the second state agrees, while the third state's ranking reverses. Each full version can have its own connected graph and zero cycle sums; an uncalibrated cross-version connection is still missing. [S9, S32]

The converse holds at the same scope: if the pulled-back potentials differ by a constant on a component, every corresponding edge within it has the same increment. Hence a cross-version check can be reduced to a forest once both versions' exactness and the state correspondence are established. This reduces the mathematical comparison burden without replacing semantic validation. If the meaning of a state, loss, unit or target has changed, equality of the numbers is not sameness of the standard. An omitted transition can also invalidate a claimed whole-graph certificate without contradicting any earlier finite calculation. Actual truth calibration, function, authority and uptake therefore remain the separate questions in §7.2.


### 7.4 Corrective compatibility, norm selection and intellectual ownership

The distinction between exact correction and warranted correction can be sharpened by the common-repair theorems. They establish a genuine restriction on possible standards. Under the fixed additive, positive-curvature assumptions on a categorical simplex with at least three outcomes, common local repair at every interior baseline forces the generators to agree up to positive scale and affine terms. This conclusion is not inserted as a premise: it follows from their shared tangent projection and the way distinct coordinates can vary across coherent contexts. Even the universal availability of a strict common local nudge has that consequence in this setting. [Common Repair, §§4.2 and 5.2](../mathematics/common-repair-and-geometric-obstructions.md#42-a-categorical-rigidity-theorem)

The operational requirement itself still needs justification. Objective correctness, coherence and the claim that each actual standard permits some correction do not entail that one correction must work across every unresolved standard. The demand for universally available standard-blind correction is stronger. Once independently defended, it excludes a mathematically specified class of incompatible pluralities and narrows the normative question. Its force should neither be erased as a mere restatement nor enlarged into a derivation of every normative premise. The source comparison develops this distinction, including the positive and negative consequences below. [S33]

#### What a compatibility class fixes

The classification does not select the actual score. Every singleton class passes a common-repair requirement. On a general finite hull, the higher-rank theorem permits different positive scales on different constrained dependency blocks and leaves free coordinates unrestricted. Those freedoms can change comparisons among coherent alternatives while preserving the same local corrective access.

For an explicit example, take a product of two three-outcome simplices with truth $`(e_1,e_1)`$ and loss


```math
L_a((x,y),(v,w))=a_1\|x-v\|^2+a_2\|y-w\|^2,
\qquad a_1,a_2>0.
```


The coherent reports $`(e_2,e_1)`$ and $`(e_1,e_2)`$ have losses $`2a_1`$ and $`2a_2`$. Their ranking reverses when the relative block scales reverse. Within each block the tangent projection is unchanged by positive scaling, so the common local repair condition persists. Corrective compatibility has constrained the geometry without fixing every normative tradeoff. A separately warranted symmetry or substantive score axiom can impose further restrictions; that extra warrant remains part of the argument.

The scope is equally important on the positive side. If every member of an arbitrary family has universal joint correctability with one fixed anchor under the higher-rank block theorem's hypotheses, the anchor projection repairs them all. The conclusion follows from the exact structural relation to that anchor. It does not follow from an abstract principle that pairwise intersecting repair sets have a common point. Binary constrained blocks have their own classification, and the three-outcome proof cannot be imported merely by making three encoded copies of a two-state problem. [Common Repair, §§4.4–6](../mathematics/common-repair-and-geometric-obstructions.md#44-the-general-higher-rank-theorem)

Nor can context identity be omitted. A family of context-dependent generators can agree with a fixed score near each local baseline without being one fixed globally equivalent generator. The earlier temporal-transport result similarly requires an independently warranted correspondence of states and units. Under an injective affine encoding, transporting the score by composition preserves its Bregman comparisons; duplicated coordinate weights add, while perturbations off the encoded image introduce a new problem. These are concrete restrictions on applying a norm across contexts. They do not by themselves identify the norm with anyone's intellectual state.

#### Causal access to a norm and authority for it

For the binary weighted quadratic family, the coherent projection of an input $`(x,y)`$ has first coordinate


```math
t=\alpha x+(1-\alpha)(1-y),
\qquad c=(t,1-t).
```


At $`(x,y)=(1/2+\varepsilon,1/2)`$ this becomes
$`t=1/2+\alpha\varepsilon`$. An exact projection therefore determines $`\alpha`$, and a merely accepted repair supplies the less exact norm readout proved in [Common Repair, §2.4](../mathematics/common-repair-and-geometric-obstructions.md#24-exact-binary-repair-cells-and-the-information-they-encode). Increasingly precise correction requires correspondingly adequate dependence on the norm parameter. Two models with identical complete available input and private-randomness laws cannot make the same controller satisfy disjoint repair requirements.

A complete algebraic implementation uses two input channels with gains $`\alpha`$ and $`1-\alpha`$, carrying $`x`$ and $`1-y`$, and a summing operation. This shows how the parameter can enter a correction mechanism through its constitution. It does not require an internally stored sentence declaring the norm correct. A physical gain network is only an idealized realization of that algebra; exact real arithmetic is not a claim about the precision of an actual device.

Two further questions remain. Choosing $`\alpha`$ after the output solely to make that output count as correct changes the target contract and supplies no correction under the original unresolved standards. Fixing $`\alpha`$ as a causally effective parameter does explain the output, but its presence alone does not justify its normative authority. A source account must explain why that parameter is the correct standard and, if relevant, whose understanding it expresses. An impersonal proposal that supplies the same causal alignment must be assessed with that full structure granted, rather than refuted by deleting its parameter and observing that the impoverished mechanism fails.

#### Endogenous functional standards and their explanatory burden

Organizational theories offer a substantive candidate for making some standards nonarbitrary within a system. Mossio, Saborido and Moreno connect a trait's contribution to maintaining an organization with the organization's production or maintenance of that trait. Their account distinguishes a trait needed in a particular maintenance regime from one indispensable across the available regimes. The resulting functional assessment must be evaluated at those organizational and environmental conditions; extension to epistemic or moral authority requires a further argument. [P29]

Kolchinsky and Wolpert instead quantify the causal relevance of system–environment correlations by comparing viability under interventions that scramble them. Their negative-entropy proposal retains an analyst-specified viability function, system boundary, initial distribution and timescale. They explicitly distinguish low entropy from preserving a particular identity and discuss alternative viability measures. This provides a precise intervention-based account of information relevance while leaving its application to original understanding and categorical authority open. [P30]

These accounts change the comparison required of an intellectual-source argument. It must explain why internally maintained, nonaccidental corrective organization is inadequate for the intended kind of representation or authority. The mere availability of a mathematical description is not such an explanation. Conversely, calling the organization endogenous does not settle which of its criteria is warranted. For any chosen real $`r`$, the update


```math
\alpha_{t+1}=\frac{\alpha_t+r}{2}
\quad\text{has}\quad
\alpha_t-r=2^{-t}(\alpha_0-r).
```


Each choice of $`r`$ yields a unique globally attracting fixed point. Convergence and uniqueness therefore leave the choice of warranted target undecided. If a further criterion justifies the update law, that criterion belongs in the explanation.

Mutual maintenance must also be typed through time. The dynamics $`x_{t+1}=y_t`$ and $`y_{t+1}=x_t`$, under suitable external conditions, exhibit a causal feedback relation between successive states. They do not say that one particular state wholly produces or authorizes itself in the same respect. A prohibition on complete same-token self-grounding cannot be applied to cross-time organization merely because both descriptions use a circular diagram. The substantive grounding argument must identify which dependence is alleged and what would make it vicious.

A limited result constrains an attempted ascent from survival normativity to a necessary ground. Suppose an action is valued solely by the probability that its bearer continues to exist. If every admitted intervention preserves that existence with probability one, every action receives value one. Such a value function cannot distinguish truthful from false representation or fitting from unfitting selection. When necessary existence is invoked, this argument applies only if the admitted interventions lie within the modal range of that necessity claim. A richer criterion concerning a particular organization, flourishing, fitting relations or effects on dependent beings adds substantive content.

This is not a refutation of a negative-entropy viability measure, which is not the same function as bare existence. Nor does it refute an account that independently grounds goodness in a necessary nature. It identifies a missing premise in the narrower inference from necessary survival alone to substantive wisdom. Biological function, identity preservation and the normative character of an ultimate ground cannot be interchanged without argument.

#### Original understanding and the identity of its bearer

Semantic nonderivativeness and causal independence concern different relations. An original intentional state, in the relevant sense, does not acquire all of its aboutness from another subject's interpretation. That does not entail that its bearer is uncaused or metaphysically independent. Searle's distinction between intrinsic and derived intentionality explicitly combines intrinsic intentional states with their causal realization in biological organisms. His position supplies a concrete rival to treating a causal-to-semantic transfer principle as common ground. [P31]

A serious alternative can consequently grant actual thinkers, original understanding and correct norm-sensitive action while placing those states in dependent subjects. It owes an adequate realization and grounding account. A positive intellectual-source argument owes a relation making the relevant content a state of the proposed ultimate bearer itself, rather than merely an effect sustained in another being. The common-repair theorem supplies neither theory merely by describing the precision of a successful correction.

There is nevertheless a valid positive hosting conditional. Let the intended modal range be fixed. Assume that one and the same representational particular $`r`$ exists throughout that range; that $`r`$ essentially has one constitutive intellectual host $`h`$; and that hosting $`r`$ entails $`h`$'s existence. Then $`h`$ exists throughout the range. The proof applies the latter two premises at every world at which the first guarantees $`r`$. The conclusion is necessary existence relative to that range.

The work lies in the premises and their identity conditions. A necessary normative truth, a proposition type, an everywhere available repair law and one necessarily existing representational particular are different objects. Even $`\forall w\,\exists r`$ with the required content does not give $`\exists r\,\forall w`$ for one particular. An independently identified host must also be shown to be the same being as an independently proposed ultimate ground. Those steps cannot be obtained by renaming a norm parameter or by repeating a universal theorem.

Minimal subjecthood should be preserved when it has actually been established. If a defended constitutive account makes the admitted original understanding a subject's understanding, an assignment that grants precisely that understanding while denying every subject is inconsistent with the account. But this modest positive conclusion does not yet establish practical agency, unrestricted knowledge, stable wisdom, truthful disclosure or the identity of a revealed source. Those are further claims, addressed by their distinct arguments elsewhere in this work.

#### What would advance the source argument

The repaired mathematical interface makes the remaining philosophical alternatives sharper. A constitutive argument could show that the selected objective content is intrinsically representational in the proposed ground and requires an intellectual host. A bearer-identity argument could connect an independently established host to that ground. A common intervention could distinguish uptake or ownership at the relevant bearer and episode, provided both candidates have warranted implementation maps for the same manipulation. An abductive comparison could show why intellectual hosting, normative integration and efficacious selection explain the actual facts better than impersonal essential structure together with dependent minds. Such a comparison must compare explanatory laws and unresolved premises, not merely count predicates. Finally, a source-conditional argument can proceed from authenticated content, identified reference and warranted recipient uptake while preserving that conditional scope. [S33]

An experiment that changes information about $`\alpha`$ is not automatically an intervention on original understanding or on acting because an outcome is fitting. It may establish the causal access requirement while leaving those interpretations alike. Conversely, the strengthened corrective geometry is genuine explanatory material for an affirmative source argument: it narrows the structures that could realize integrated correction and prevents an incompatible standard plurality from being assumed harmless. The further intellectual, teleological and source arguments in §§4–6 and 9–12 must be assessed with those gains and these remaining obligations both intact.

<a id="r359-bz-potential-evidence"></a>

### 7.5 Exact cycle evidence, ordinal improvement and a counting discrepancy

The exact potential in this section has stronger requirements than an ordering of preferred revisions. On the underlying finite graph, choose an orientation for each edge and let the weight of traversing an edge backwards be the negative of its forward weight. Exactness means that a scalar $`V`$ satisfies $`w(u,v)=V(v)-V(u)`$ on every registered edge. The sum along any closed walk is then zero by telescoping. Conversely, if every closed walk has zero sum, choose one reference vertex in each connected component and define $`V(v)`$ by a path sum from that reference. Two paths give the same value because one followed by the reverse of the other is a closed walk. The potential is determined up to one additive constant per component. [R359BZ-N]

A spanning forest gives an exact finite certificate of this condition. The tree-edge weights determine a candidate potential from the chosen component origins. Each non-tree edge then closes a fundamental cycle; agreement on those edges is equivalent to zero sum on all cycles. This is path-independence evidence, not merely the absence of forward directed cycles. A directed acyclic orientation can still have two paths between the same vertices whose assigned increments disagree. A topological or ordinal rank can order those vertices while failing to realize their specified increments exactly.

The source's perturbation controls make the distinction concrete. Changing the weight of a bridge can be absorbed by shifting the potential on one side of the cut; no cycle constraint crosses that bridge and returns by another route. Changing only one edge on a cycle can instead destroy exactness because the remaining route fixes its required difference. Coordinated changes to several cycle weights can preserve exactness, so the conclusion is about the stated perturbation, not every alteration of a cyclic network. These controls concern a supplied edge registry and its complete comparisons.

Neither an exact potential nor an ordinal rank calibrates itself to truth or normative correctness. An arbitrary numerical target can yield a perfectly exact edge field. The calibration and authority conditions in §§7.2–7.4 remain separate from the algebraic certificate. Likewise, a common potential does not identify one intellectual bearer or establish that an agent selects a revision because it is fitting. Those stronger conclusions need the evidence appropriate to their respective targets.

The recorded counting history contains an unresolved discrepancy that must accompany the positive theorem. The admission-metadata repair rejects a total of 59,049 and corrects the declared six-coordinate, four-choice domain to $`4^6=4,096`$. The distinct-review prose nevertheless reports 4,096 total cases and 7,425 exact cases. The latter cannot be a subset of that same printed domain. Its structured spanning-tree/holonomy result separately records 1,040 exact cases. The successor preserves the incompatible prose declaration and that structured value; it does not invent a different enumeration, attribute the discrepancy to a specific programming error, or claim to have rerun the test. [R359BZ-N]

The source also retains seven repairs and separate reported populations of 30,000 exact and 30,000 perturbed cases. They are historical evidence at their stated scope. The theorem is supported here by the path-sum argument under its explicit hypotheses, while the inconsistent count remains a limitation of the recorded report. The mathematical ancestry is the familiar graph-potential and cycle-exactness criterion, not a new general theorem of truth-directed cognition. [R359BZ-N]



The registry is finite and static at the declared scope, with stable labels and all relevant registered routes included. Omitted transitions, hidden state or target drift require a new application of the criterion. Distinct connected components retain independent additive constants, so their potentials are not comparable without a further calibration. Within one exact potential, a directed cycle of strictly positive increments is impossible: telescoping would make its positive sum zero. This consequence is narrower than saying that every acyclic improvement relation realizes a preassigned cardinal scale. The original audit also distinguished a primary check that reused its solver from the later spanning-tree method; agreement of two calls to the same solver was not counted as independent corroboration. [BZ-N02] [BZ-N04] [BZ-N06]

<a id="receipt44-calibration-review"></a>

#### Calibration certificates and their proper-function limits

The forest-and-cycle theorem of §§7–7.2 and the exact-potential argument above already cover the mathematical content of the later calibration review. Its contribution here is to preserve the checking history and the limits of that theorem, not to repeat the proof as another origin. With the convention $`d(u,v)=L(u)-L(v)`$, a calibrated potential has the form $`N=-L+c_C`$ on each connected component $`C`$. An exact potential with the opposite orientation can exist while rewarding increased truth loss. Zero cycle sums alone do not warrant the truth or authority of $`L`$, and an ordinal ranking does not reproduce prescribed cardinal increments. These distinctions require the independently fixed truth-linked loss and the static complete registry already stated in this work. [R44-BH02] [R44-BH04]

The historical primary harness first stopped with a key lookup error caused by inconsistent orientation of a stored edge. The repair used an antisymmetric edge accessor and restarted the check before freezing its successful version. The recorded failure concerns that implementation, not a refutation of the graph theorem; the receipt does not license treating the failed run as a PASS. The later distinct review used componentwise path integration and reports **1,728** exhaustive three-vertex graph/field/loss cases, **60,000** possibly disconnected cases and **1,024** proper-function account expansions, together with the countermodel in which a newly admitted edge invalidates a formerly valid certificate. No checker was executed for this incorporation. [R44-BH01] [R44-BH03]

The account expansions support a signature-relative nonentailment only. If the reduct omits an account's function-fixing relation, varying a Boolean function label shows what that reduct leaves open. It does not show that the label remains free after selected history, design, organization, teleology or an authenticated source purpose has been supplied. Nor does success at reducing a true loss establish the corresponding fixing relation. Selection or design can concern a non-truth-directed aim; a truth-linked proper-function conclusion needs the appropriate truth aim as an additional warranted premise. A Plantingian account also invokes its design-plan and environment conditions, while a fiṭrah account remains dependent on the particular source, school, translation and applicability claims. The ten audit repairs preserve these account-specific requirements. The ordinary graph-potential mechanism has no independent general-novelty claim, and the finite checks confer no external-review or source-authentication status. [R44-BH02] [R44-BH03] [R44-BH04]

<a id="receipt68-calibration-records"></a>

#### The finite calibration populations

The primary calibration file reports **50,000** random cases, **30,000** exact cases, **16,419** perturbed cases and **1,024** account-flag assignments. The distinct review reports **144,014** component-constant cases alongside **1,728** exhaustive cases, **60,000** random cases and **1,024** account assignments. These are the files' separately defined populations, not additive trials of one fixed design. Their account flags and coherence checks concern the stipulated relation among evaluator, account and calibration conditions. They do not establish the truth of an account, the authority of a source or the independent warrant of a recipient. The proof and qualification above remain the scientific claim; these records identify its finite checking history and the boundary of what those checks say. [R68-S046] [R68-S047]

## 8. What interventions can distinguish

A functional explanation may have consequences that ordinary observation cannot distinguish. A useful intervention must discriminate the competing explanations through the complete response relevant to the inquiry. Merely distinguishing a surface action can leave its target, source attribution, or normative mode unsettled. [S10]

Let $`H`$ be a finite nonempty hypothesis set and $`I`$ a finite set of authorized, feasible interventions. Let $`O`$ be the response domain, and suppose

```math
r:H\times I\longrightarrow O
\quad\text{(8)}\quad
```

is deterministic and static for the experiment in question. A response can be a structured object rather than a single bit. The model assumes that each hypothesis and each intervention retain their meaning during the inquiry; state-changing tests require a richer transition model.

For distinct hypotheses define the disagreement set

```math
\Delta(h,h')=\{i\in I:r(h,i)\ne r(h',i)\}.
\quad\text{(9)}\quad
```

**Proposition 4 (nonadaptive discrimination).** A set $`J\subseteq I`$ identifies every hypothesis from its response vector if and only if $`J`$ meets every disagreement set $`\Delta(h,h')`$ for distinct $`h,h'`$.

**Proof.** If $`J`$ misses a disagreement set, the two associated hypotheses have the same response at every selected intervention, so their response vectors coincide. They are not identified. Conversely, if every disagreement set is met, every pair of hypotheses differs in at least one selected coordinate. All response vectors are distinct, so observing the complete vector identifies its hypothesis within $`H`$. $`\square`$

Thus the minimum number of nonadaptive tests is the minimum size of a hitting set for the pairwise disagreement sets. If a disagreement set is empty, no permitted nonadaptive test set distinguishes that pair. Static determinism also means that adaptivity cannot distinguish an observationally identical pair.

### 8.1 The exact adaptive recurrence

For a current nonempty candidate set $`S\subseteq H`$ and unused interventions $`J\subseteq I`$, let $`V(S,J)`$ be the minimum possible worst-case number of further interventions needed for exact identification. Set $`V(S,J)=0`$ when $`|S|=1`$. For an intervention $`i`$, its response classes are

```math
S_{i,o}=\{h\in S:r(h,i)=o\}.
\quad\text{(10)}\quad
```

Call $`i`$ splitting when at least two of these classes are nonempty. For $`|S|>1`$,

```math
V(S,J)=
1+\min_{\substack{i\in J\\ i\ \mathrm{splits}\ S}}
\ \max_{\substack{o\in O\\S_{i,o}\ne\varnothing}}
V\bigl(S_{i,o},J\setminus\{i\}\bigr),
\quad\text{(11)}\quad
```

where a minimum over no splitting intervention is interpreted as $`\infty`$ and $`1+\infty=\infty`$.

**Proposition 5 (adaptive optimality).** Under the stated finite, deterministic, static model, equation (11) gives the optimal worst-case number of tests.

**Proof.** A test that does not split the current candidate set conveys no identifying information and can be removed from a decision tree without worsening its performance. Repeating a previously used test is likewise useless because its response is already fixed on the current candidate set.

Any successful nontrivial decision tree therefore begins with a splitting intervention $`i`$. After observing $`o`$, it must identify the actual hypothesis within $`S_{i,o}`$ using the remaining interventions. Its worst-case depth is at least one plus the largest optimal depth among those nonempty branches. Minimizing over possible first tests gives the lower bound in equation (11).

Conversely, choose a minimizing first intervention and an optimal subtree for each nonempty response class. The resulting decision tree attains that value. The argument is an induction on the number of remaining interventions; terminal singleton sets have value zero, and a nonsingleton set without a possible split cannot be identified. Finite minima are attained because the intervention set is finite. $`\square`$

### 8.2 A strict adaptive advantage

Consider four hypotheses and three binary interventions:

| Hypothesis | $`i_0`$ | $`i_1`$ | $`i_2`$ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| $`h_0`$ | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| $`h_1`$ | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| $`h_2`$ | 0 | 1 | 0 |
| $`h_3`$ | 1 | 0 | 1 |

All three interventions are needed nonadaptively. Omitting $`i_0`$ leaves $`h_1`$ and $`h_3`$ identical; omitting $`i_1`$ leaves $`h_0`$ and $`h_2`$ identical; omitting $`i_2`$ leaves $`h_0`$ and $`h_1`$ identical.

Two tests suffice adaptively. First use $`i_2`$. If its result is zero, the candidates are $`h_0,h_2`$ and $`i_1`$ distinguishes them. If its result is one, the candidates are $`h_1,h_3`$ and $`i_0`$ distinguishes them. A binary tree of depth one has at most two leaves, so one test cannot identify four hypotheses. The adaptive optimum is therefore exactly two, while the nonadaptive optimum is exactly three.

Adding permitted interventions cannot increase the optimum, because the old procedure is still available. Removing interventions cannot improve it. These monotonicity claims hold only when the hypotheses, response semantics, authorization, and target remain fixed. Changing what a response means changes the experiment.

#### Why the first query and the remaining test set matter

The intervention study's [cold audit][receipt-as-02] identified two concrete defects despite the primary finite record's `PASS`. Its displayed recurrence retained the full intervention set at every child, leaving the stated recursion insufficiently specified; the repaired version carries the unused set and removes the selected test. Its explanation of the four-row example also named the wrong first query. Querying the first coordinate leaves three candidates on one branch, whereas the third coordinate gives the two branches exhibited above. The [repair log][receipt-as-04] records both changes. The proof here explicitly justifies removing non-splitting and repeated tests, and the example gives the corrected depth-two tree. A successful computation does not validate a different displayed recurrence or an incorrect explanatory tree.

The other six findings delimit what such a tree identifies. The response must retain every relevant certificate coordinate, including source, reason and warrant, unless a sufficient projection is separately established. Interventions must be feasible and authorized, with a fixed hidden account, target, source/version, environment and response meaning. Identifying an account within the catalogue does not show that it is true or normatively adequate, or that the catalogue contains the actual account. A truth-divergence test needs an independently warranted truth coordinate; putting the desired answer into a response label cannot supply it. Stochastic, bounded-error, state-changing or history-dependent models require their own contracts. The hitting-set and decision-tree facts are standard results, with no claim of general mathematical novelty. [Cold audit][receipt-as-02]; [repair record][receipt-as-04].

The [primary results][receipt-as-06] report exhaustive examination of 65,536 binary four-account, four-intervention matrices: 43,680 were identifiable, 21,856 were not, and 16,128 had a strict adaptive advantage. They also report 262,144 intervention-deletion monotonicity cases and no recorded failure. The [distinct rereview][receipt-as-03] used partition refinement, pairwise-disagreement hitting sets and a bitmask decision-tree computation. Its [result record][receipt-as-05] separates 19,683 exhaustive ternary three-by-three matrices from 50,000 sampled binary five-by-five matrices: together 53,693 were identifiable, 15,990 were not and 3,070 had a strict adaptive gap. Six frozen files matched, the surface-projection control passed and no failure was reported. The two populations have different alphabets and sizes; their counts are evidence for their stated implementations, not one exhaustive larger experiment or a substitute for the proofs.

The [admission record][receipt-as-01] is limited to the finite deterministic static identification result. It leaves empirical adequacy, catalogue completeness, source authentication, a personal designer, wisdom and wider metaphysical conclusions unestablished, with external review open and owner adoption pending. No original checker is rerun here. In particular, the subsequent changing-state experiment in this work has a separate transition, information and initial-law argument; these static response-matrix checks do not qualify that richer construction. The positive conclusion remains useful and precise: under a warranted response contract, the formulas identify the best tests for the distinctions that contract actually contains.


### 8.3 The epistemic boundary

The finite results do not choose the hypothesis class, warrant the assumed response map, or prove that an authorized intervention is ethically or practically permissible in a real setting. Those are input conditions, not consequences of the combinatorics. A finite class may omit the actual mechanism or a hybrid of its members. Stochastic responses require probabilistic identification criteria, not a deterministic decision-tree certificate relabeled as empirical certainty.

A richer response may include the operative target, the claimed source, reasons offered, and the force of an address. Projecting that response to a surface action loses whatever distinctions are not constant on the projection's fibres, by Proposition 2. A benign and a malicious mechanism can agree in an aligned setting; a version change can alter a source's response contract; an intervention can leak the disputed target into the result by construction. These are reasons to specify the experiment accurately, not to abandon intervention as evidence.

The constructive conclusion is exact: once an eligible finite response model is warranted, the proof supplies both the nonadaptive criterion and an optimal adaptive procedure. The philosophical conclusion is equally exact: discriminatory behavior establishes only the distinctions the response model genuinely carries.

#### A common experiment with changing states

The preceding static examples can be supplemented by an explicit experiment whose interventions change the system. Its purpose is to show exactly how a shared transition and observation contract can leave personal and impersonal interpretations unresolved, even when the next intervention depends on earlier observations. The construction specializes the information-preserving controller argument in [Collective Inquiry and Strategic Coordination, §§7.5–7.6](../mathematics/collective-inquiry-and-strategic-coordination.md#75-transferring-a-restorative-controller-without-a-hidden-state-oracle); it supplies a concrete application, not another general transfer theorem. [S28]

Let the neutral state space be $`N=\{0,1\}^6`$, with coordinates

```math
x=(c,f,g,v,a,r).
```

These coordinates record content, a fittingness signal, a payoff report, version validity, authority validity, and a provenance-root bit. They are the experiment's stipulated registers. In particular, the fittingness signal is not an independent proof of objective fittingness, and the validity bits do not authenticate a source merely by being called valid. There are seven actions: $`0`$ does nothing, and $`j\in\{1,\ldots,6\}`$ toggles coordinate $`j`$. With $`e_j`$ the corresponding unit vector and addition modulo two, define

```math
T_0(x)=x,\qquad T_j(x)=x+e_j,
\qquad O(x)=(c+f,\ g,\ va,\ r).
```

The four outputs are the decision bit, payoff report, joint version-and-authority signal, and provenance report. An output is emitted after each action; the empty action sequence produces no output. This timing matters when assessing a proposed discriminator.

For each label $`h\in\{u,i,p\}`$, take $`X_h=N\times\{h\}`$, projection $`\rho_h(x,h)=x`$, transition $`T_j^h(x,h)=(T_j(x),h)`$, and output $`O_h(x,h)=O(x)`$. The additional target predicates have the following stipulated values:

| Interpretation | Common bearer | Intentional uptake | Selection because fitting | Strong wise-agency package |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Personal $`u`$ | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 |
| One-bearer impersonal $`i`$ | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Plural coordinated $`p`$ | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

All three receive the same functional descriptions for knowledge, selection, efficacy, and semantic response. In the plural description, the roles are assigned to distinct bearers with a coordinating role. These are formal interpretations of a declared interface. The construction does not prove that a physical impersonal system has intentional knowledge, that a plural system is metaphysically possible, or that the additional personal predicates are true of an actual system. Its exact positive achievement is an implementation of every declared neutral action in all three formal structures without consulting any disputed target predicate:

```math
\rho_h\circ T_j^h=T_j\circ\rho_h,
\qquad O_h=O\circ\rho_h.
```

Both equalities follow directly by evaluating their two sides at $`(x,h)`$. Thus the transition commutes with projection at every state and action, and the observation meanings are identical.

Now fix the same initial neutral state and the same policy in each interpretation. At time $`t`$, the policy may use the complete preceding sequence of actions and outputs, but cannot read $`h`$ or an unobserved target predicate. The projected trajectories and observable histories coincide at every finite time. At time zero the projections are equal and the histories are empty. If they agree through time $`t`$, the common policy chooses the same next action; the commuting transitions give the same next projected state; and the common output map gives the same next observation. This proves the claim by induction. The step works at every finite time, so its scope is not restricted to a previously enumerated finite list of action words.

A behavioral randomized policy has the same conclusion in law when each interpretation starts with the same full distribution on $`N`$ and uses the same next-action distribution at each common history. Couple the initial states identically and use the same fresh current-action draw at equal histories. The preceding induction holds on every resulting coupled finite trajectory, and each marginal follows its specified policy and initial law. Hence the complete finite action/output laws agree. This is the deterministic-plant, identical-interface specialization of the already established full-law transfer argument. It neither supplies a new infinite-horizon theorem nor transfers a policy allowed to consult different private information. An already populated controller memory must likewise be matched, rather than discarded without justification.

The initial-state condition is substantive. A run starting at $`(0,0,0,0,0,0)`$ and one starting at $`(1,0,0,0,0,0)`$ give different decision outputs after a no-op, despite sharing all transition and output formulas. Equality of formulas alone does not equalize different initial laws. The declared resource condition is similarly narrow: a plant action changes at most one bit. It is not a bound on the policy's memory, the cost of establishing source authority, or the whole inquiry.

Under the matched conditions, no exact decoder of a common finite observation can recover an architecture label or a target coordinate that varies among the three expansions. Equal observations would have to receive incompatible decoded values, contrary to Proposition 2. For the randomized version, the same obstruction applies to an almost-sure exact classifier under the common history law: disjoint output labels cannot each have probability one under that same law. A supplied common-bearer value can separate the plural case from the other two, but that value is an additional coordinate, not an output of this kernel. None of these conclusions erases richer evidence for wise agency or a source argument whose independently warranted premises exceed this experiment.

#### Controls that change the contract

Three concrete changes show why target independence, exact transition meaning, and the complete intervention menu matter. [S28]

First, append $`h`$ to every emitted output. Every nonempty word then identifies the label by construction; the empty word still emits nothing. This is an answer-reading oracle unless an independent observation procedure warrants the extra value. A policy can introduce the same defect before the readout: choosing a content flip only when $`h=u`$ and a no-op when $`h=i`$ uses information the common policy was forbidden to consult. A resulting difference is evidence about the changed interface.

Second, let the plural implementation of “flip version” toggle the authority bit instead. Its projected next state now disagrees with the declared transition at every initial state. The disagreement is observationally visible at only half of the sixty-four states under the four-coordinate readout. For example, from $`(0,0,0,0,1,0)`$ the intended update produces $`v=a=1`$ and reports eligibility one; the substituted update produces $`v=a=0`$ and reports zero. In general the intended and substituted eligibility reports are $`(1-v)a`$ and $`v(1-a)`$, which differ exactly when $`v\ne a`$. A state-commutation failure and an observed-output difference are distinct counts.

Third, keep the common transitions but alter the impersonal readout so that, immediately after a provenance-bit flip, its decision bit is complemented. After every other action it uses the original readout. An experiment whose action words never contain that flip agrees exactly with the original personal implementation; one provenance-bit flip distinguishes the two from every initial state. This altered observation rule violates the original common-output contract. It demonstrates how omitting an intervention can hide a difference without contradicting the equal-contract result.

Other proposed operations require a different kind of qualification. The construction supplies no common, meaning-preserving implementation of “toggle ownership” across one-bearer and plural interpretations. It supplies no neutral operation for asking whether a source's intensional predicate is true, querying divine speech, or selecting the actual world. Those omissions establish a boundary of this declared experiment. They do not prove that no independently warranted observation, source application, or enlarged experiment could ever distinguish the candidates. Such an addition must have its own implementation, interpretation, and eligibility argument.

### 8.4 Identification under independently eligible evidence

The preceding discrimination results leave a further question: which distinctions may the inquiry legitimately use? A description can assign an informative coordinate to every candidate without supplying any independently warranted way to obtain or interpret it. Writing “the true architecture” in a table would separate the alternatives perfectly. It would do no evidential work. Conversely, independently warranted testimony can add a distinction that a deliberately restricted behavioral experiment omits. The formal question of separation and the epistemic question of eligibility must therefore be joined without identifying them. [S23–S28]

Fix a finite nonempty class of candidates $`M`$, a target map $`Q:M\to C`$, and a finite registry $`A`$ of proposed evidential coordinates, here called **anchors**. An evidence context $`k`$ supplies a subset $`A_k\subseteq A`$ whose members are independently eligible for the comparison. Each eligible anchor $`a`$ is a total map $`a:M\to V_a`$ with a fixed type, interpretation, and version. The mathematical model assumes these maps and their eligibility; it does not establish either. An observed value must have the meaning assigned by the map, and missing information must not silently be filled with the disputed target's value.

For $`S\subseteq A_k`$, write $`P_S(m)=(a(m))_{a\in S}`$ for the complete joint profile. Identification of $`Q`$ means that a function $`f:P_S(M)\to C`$ satisfies $`Q=f\circ P_S`$. The target can be coarser than complete candidate identity: different candidates may require the same action or share the same relevant property. Define the set of target-conflict pairs and the pairs covered by one anchor as

```math
E_Q=\{\{m,m'\}\subseteq M:Q(m)\ne Q(m')\},
\qquad
C_a=\{e=\{m,m'\}\in E_Q:a(m)\ne a(m')\}.
```

The context-relative minimum is

```math
\kappa_Q(k)=\min\bigl\{|S|:S\subseteq A_k,
             \ \bigcup_{a\in S}C_a=E_Q\bigr\},
```

with value $`\infty`$ when no cover exists. The empty family is allowed. Thus a constant target has minimum zero, including when no anchor is eligible.

**Proposition 5A (eligible target-conflict cover).** A family $`S\subseteq A_k`$ identifies $`Q`$ exactly if and only if it covers every member of $`E_Q`$. Consequently, some eligible family identifies the target if and only if $`\kappa_Q(k)<\infty`$.

**Proof.** Suppose $`Q=f\circ P_S`$. If a conflict pair were uncovered, its two members would have equal values at every selected anchor and hence the same joint profile. Applying $`f`$ would give equal target values, contrary to the definition of a conflict pair. Thus every conflict pair is covered.

Conversely, suppose every conflict pair is covered. If $`P_S(m)=P_S(m')`$, no selected anchor distinguishes the two candidates. They therefore cannot form a target-conflict pair, so $`Q(m)=Q(m')`$. Assign to each attained profile the target value of any candidate producing it. The preceding implication makes the assignment well-defined, and it gives the required $`f`$. The finite registry has finitely many subsets, so any nonempty collection of covering subsets contains one of minimum cardinality. If none exists, some conflict pair is uncovered even by $`A_k`$ itself, and no eligible subfamily can identify the target. $`\square`$

This combines the fibre criterion in Proposition 2 with the finite discrimination argument of Proposition 4; it is not an independent new general theorem. What changes is the explicit eligibility condition and the possibility that several candidates have the same target value. [S26–S27]

The direction of refinement is essential. Let three candidates have target values $`(0,0,1)`$ and let an eligible anchor have values $`(x,y,z)`$, all distinct. The anchor identifies the target by sending both $`x`$ and $`y`$ to zero and $`z`$ to one. There are three attained profiles and only two target values. Requiring the numbers to be equal would reject this valid identifier. Identification requires each profile fibre to lie inside a target fibre; it does not require the two partitions to coincide. This example also shows why extra information within one target class is harmless to exact identification. [S26]

### 8.5 What additional evidence can improve

**Proposition 5B (fixed-map eligibility expansion).** Suppose the candidate class, target, and value maps remain fixed and $`A_k\subseteq A_d`$. Then

```math
\kappa_Q(d)\leq\kappa_Q(k)
```

in the extended order containing $`\infty`$.

**Proof.** Every eligible covering family in $`k`$ remains an eligible covering family in $`d`$. If the old minimum is finite, the new minimum cannot exceed the cardinality of an old minimizing family. If the old value is infinite, the inequality holds automatically in the extended order. This includes a constant target, for which both minima are zero. $`\square`$

The map-preservation hypothesis cannot be replaced by preservation of a name. On two candidates with different targets, an old anchor with values $`(0,1)`$ identifies the target. A later same-named instrument with values $`(0,0)`$ does not. Counting the latter as the former would falsely infer monotonicity from a change of semantics. A new candidate population can likewise introduce a previously absent conflict. Eligibility expansion, instrument revision, and enlargement of the comparison class are different operations. [S26]

The minimum counts entries in the declared registry. It is not a measure of epistemic strength, acquisition effort, proof length, or explanatory simplicity. If two binary anchors are repackaged as one ordered-pair anchor, the same information can have registry cardinality one instead of two. A weighted variant may minimize $`\sum_{a\in S}w(a)`$ for independently specified nonnegative costs, but the choice and additivity of those costs require their own justification. A source package containing many conditions must not be advertised as one cheap observation because the table gives it one column. Actual inquiry, possible failed checks, and warranted human uptake remain separate questions.

A small architecture comparison illustrates both the constructive result and its limitation. Let the target distinguish a unified personal candidate $`u`$, a single-bearer impersonal candidate $`i`$, and a plural coordinated candidate $`p`$. These are stipulated comparison descriptions, not a proof that any description is actual or metaphysically realizable. The columns below are the complete value maps used by this example. [S26–S28]

| Candidate | Declared neutral profile | Common-bearer coordinate | Intentional-uptake coordinate | Guarded source package |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| $`u`$ | $`n`$ | 1 | 1 | 1 |
| $`i`$ | $`n`$ | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| $`p`$ | $`n`$ | 0 | 0 | 0 |

With only the neutral column eligible, no target-conflict pair is covered and the minimum is infinite. If the two formal coordinates are admitted, they jointly give profiles $`(1,1)`$, $`(1,0)`$, and $`(0,0)`$, so they identify the three candidates. Neither alone suffices: common bearer leaves $`u,i`$ together; uptake leaves $`i,p`$ together. The minimum is exactly two in that registry. The common-bearer values are input to this calculation. No bearer-identity or property-ontology theorem is being inferred from them; those questions belong to the separate treatment in [Received Actuality and Intrinsic Necessity, §10.10](received-actuality-and-intrinsic-necessity.md#1010-what-isand-is-notunified-by-the-construction).

The guarded source column induces the same partition as uptake in this table. If that package and the common-bearer coordinate are independently eligible in a source-conditioned comparison, their minimum is also two. Equality of partitions does not make their warrants interchangeable. In particular, the formal-coordinate calculation supplies no operational procedure for measuring uptake, and the source-conditioned calculation supplies no neutral measurement of it.

Adding a column with values $`(u,i,p)`$ would reduce the minimum to one. If the column merely reads the answer under dispute, its exclusion is an eligibility judgment, not a combinatorial defect. If an independently warranted experiment genuinely has those three responses, its admission would instead be a substantive improvement. The result leaves that possibility open. Likewise, if independently warranted premises reduce the candidate class to $`\{u\}`$, the residual identification minimum becomes zero. That does not mean that establishing those premises required no evidence.

The common-bearer coordinate in this example has a deliberately limited type: co-instantiation of the required roles in one bearer within one world. Its value does not by itself assert uniqueness, identity across worlds, identity of an intensional source referent, derivational unification, or personality. The separate incidence argument determines when co-instantiation is available; the present comparison uses its value as an input. [S27]

If an independently warranted background fixes that value at one, the exact residual class in this table is $`\{u,i\}`$. The remaining task is to warrant a target-independent, type-preserving anchor or enlarged experiment that distinguishes those two, or to show that a specified admissible family leaves their conflict uncovered. Writing “intentional uptake” as a coordinate identifies where a distinction would help; it does not operationalize its measurement. The full source package can supply the required distinction conditionally on its own source and world premises. Neither this conditional reduction nor perfect classification within the table proves that the three candidates exhaust the actual possibilities. Without the common-bearer premise, the plural candidate also remains in the declared neutral fibre. [S27–S28]

<a id="marginal-evidence-gain-and-private-witnesses"></a>

#### Useful evidence, indispensable evidence and an optimal cover

The cover theorem also distinguishes three questions: whether a factor adds information, whether a selected cover needs it, and whether that cover is smallest. Fix the finite candidate class, target, independently eligible registry and complete value maps of §8.4. Keep a base evidence profile E fixed. Let C be its unordered cross-target collision pairs, and let $`C_f\subseteq C`$ be the pairs separated by an eligible factor f. For a selected family S define

```math
U(S)=\bigcup_{f\in S}C_f,
\qquad G(S)=|U(S)|,
\qquad \Delta_f(S)=G(S\cup\{f\})-G(S).
```

The residual collision set is exactly $`C\setminus U(S)`$: a base-colliding pair remains indistinguishable precisely when every added factor agrees on it. Its cardinality is $`|C|-G(S)`$. Thus zero residual gives identification on the attained joint-profile image by Proposition 5A, without extending the decoder to unattained profiles. These quantities count conflicting pairs, not probabilities, acquisition costs or epistemic warrant. [GAIN-R8] [GAIN-R10] [GAIN-INTAKE]

**Marginal gain and diminishing returns.** The gain is monotone, and for $`S\subseteq T`$,

```math
\Delta_f(S)=|C_f\setminus U(S)|
\ \ge\ |C_f\setminus U(T)|=\Delta_f(T).
```

**Proof.** Adjoining f adds exactly its pairs not already in the union. Enlarging the selected family enlarges that union and can only shrink this difference. This proves monotonicity and the diminishing-returns form of submodularity. Overlapping coverage is counted once, not once per factor. ∎

A factor determined entirely by E separates no base collision, regardless of its range size or descriptive complexity. Conversely, a field that simply reads the disputed target can remove every collision without being independently eligible. Optimization therefore follows eligibility and a warranted common implementation; it cannot supply them. Complementary weak factors can jointly identify the target even though neither suffices alone. [GAIN-R8]

**Private-witness criterion.** Suppose S covers C and $`f\in S`$. Deleting f destroys identification exactly when

```math
C_f\setminus\bigcup_{g\in S\setminus\{f\}}C_g\ne\varnothing,
```

or equivalently $`\Delta_f(S\setminus\{f\})>0`$.

**Proof.** A pair left uncovered after deletion was covered before deletion, so f must have been its only cover within S. Conversely any such private pair remains a target collision when f is removed. ∎

Positive gain when f was first considered does not make it indispensable after other factors have been added. If every member of S has a private witness, S is inclusion-minimal; that does not establish minimum cardinality over the registry. For example, two factors covering one distinct collision each are both necessary within that two-factor cover, while a separately eligible third factor covering both would give a smaller cover. This is a conditional coverage example, not an assertion that such a measurement is available in an actual inquiry. [GAIN-R10]

Nor does maximum marginal gain guarantee even an inclusion-minimal final cover. An explicit six-collision example has coverage sets $`C_a=\{1,2,3,4\}`$, $`C_b=\{1,2,5\}`$ and $`C_c=\{3,4,6\}`$. Greedy gain selects a first, then needs both b and c to cover the remaining collisions 5 and 6. But b and c already cover all six, and no single factor does; the optimum is two. These sets can be realized by a constant base profile on one target-one and six target-zero candidates: each binary factor distinguishes exactly its listed opposite-target pairs. This displayed set system is an elementary illustration of the source's stated greedy limitation, not a recovery of its unprinted historical checker table. Deletion testing can remove redundancy from a greedy result, but does not by itself prove global minimum size.

Finally, if the union of the entire eligible registry leaves a collision, no subfamily in that registry identifies the target. This is the existing bounded no-cover conclusion, not impossibility for an enlarged justified experiment or a different candidate class. Marginal gain provides a useful selection heuristic under the fixed contract; exact minimum-cover calculation answers a different question. The source reports 32,768 three-factor signatures and 262,144 gain/residual checks, and separately 74,954 finite set systems for the cover comparison. These are historical declarations, not executions here. Its intake warns that the aggregate “79/79 mutants” included seven hard-coded scope assertions and could reuse stale outputs; those labels supply no additional proof of the displayed arguments or empirical implementation. Causal identification and source-world truth still need their own premises. [GAIN-R8] [GAIN-R10] [GAIN-INTAKE]

#### Eligible refinements need not have a unique weakest member

The eligible-anchor problem must also distinguish minimum registry size from informational weakness. For a fixed current profile $`E`$ and target $`Q`$, a candidate refinement $`r`$ suffices exactly when $`\ker(E,r)\subseteq\ker Q`$. Order candidates by their **joint** partitions with $`E`$, with a coarser partition counting as weaker. A finite nonempty family of sufficient candidates has a minimal element in this order; it need not have a least element, even after equivalent joint partitions are identified. [CPCT-CS]

For an explicit witness, let $`E`$ be constant on $`\{0,1,2,3\}`$ and let the target partition be $`\{0,1\}\mid\{2,3\}`$. Suppose the independently eligible family contains just the two refinements

```math
\{0,1\}\mid\{2\}\mid\{3\},\qquad
\{0\}\mid\{1\}\mid\{2,3\}.
```

Both determine the target and neither is coarser than the other. Each is minimal within that family, with no least eligible refinement. Among unrestricted joint partitions refining $`E`$ and determining $`Q`$, the partition of $`(E,Q)`$ is a coarsest bound. But naming $`Q`$ inside that bound is not an independent procedure for measuring it. A constant refinement is weak but can be uninformative; a target oracle is discriminating but can be ineligible. These controls replace the source's earlier claims of a universally unique weakest remedy. Its cited weakness and allocation literature remains a proof-search heuristic here, not a derivation of that selection policy. [CPCT-CS]

#### A partial experiment and an observed failure to implement it are different evidence

If an outcome $`Y`$ is supplied only on a proper domain $`D\subsetneq M`$, its on-domain record alone cannot distinguish two target assignments that agree on $`D`$ and differ outside it. This does **not** imply that every total observation involving that experiment is globally uninformative. If availability $`A=1_D`$ is itself observed under a common, independently warranted contract, the signal is

```math
X(m)=(A(m),\,Y(m)\text{ if }A(m)=1\text{, otherwise }\bot).
```

It is a new evidential coordinate, subject to the ordinary fibre criterion. For example, take a constant old profile on four candidates, target values $`(0,0,0,1)`$, availability with those same values, and constant on-domain outcome zero. The outcome is defined on only one candidate, yet the availability bit identifies the target. This refutes the earlier blanket claim that a proper domain prevents identification even after bottom totalization. It does not validate a target-laden availability assignment: if “available exactly when personal” merely reads the conclusion under dispute, the apparent discriminator is an oracle, not evidence. [CPCT-CT]

The precise unchanged-information barrier is different: if the **whole** totalized signal $`X`$ factors through the old profile $`E`$, then $`(E,X)`$ has exactly the old fibres and cannot resolve an old target conflict. Conversely, genuinely measured capability or nonimplementation can supply information not measurable from $`E`$, but only after the common semantics, implementation, version, authorization, resource and missingness conditions are warranted. A common-bearer probe can separate a plural candidate while leaving personal and one-bearer impersonal candidates together. Directly assigning an uptake-only domain does not by itself supply the missing common experiment. [CPCT-CT]

The source preserves eight explicit failures of its first blanket rule. Its repaired record separates 32,064 identifying totalizations on **nonempty proper** domains from 18,572 successful refinements of previously conflicted profiles, and reports 15,652 old-profile-measurable cases with no barrier failure. These are differently defined populations, not additive replications or evidence that the formal architecture descriptions are actual. The constructive possibility of a newly warranted observation remains open; so does the distinct force of a source argument whose independently justified premises exceed the neutral registry.


### 8.6 Source-conditioned exclusion and its exact burden

A source-based argument needs to distinguish the truth of a claim, its attribution to a source, and its application to the target being assessed. Consider a contract $`(b,\Pi,\mathrm{Auth},\mathrm{Ident})`$. Here $`b`$ is the source's intended referent, $`\Pi`$ is the relevant predicate bundle, $`\mathrm{Auth}`$ supplies the requisite warrant for accepting that attributed content, and $`\mathrm{Ident}`$ identifies its referent with the target bearer. Authentication in this sense is more than possessing a document or matching its checksum. Its sufficient epistemic conditions must be warranted for the kind of claim being made. Referent identification is equally substantive: a correct statement about one object need not be a correct statement about another. [S23–S24]

The same distinction applies to a school-internal theological reconstruction. Its source predicates may have real argumentative force under that reconstruction's warranted premises. They do not become conclusions of a deliberately source-neutral argument merely by being included in a formal model. Nor does the failure of the restricted argument cancel an independently warranted source argument. The source-conditioned comparison studied here uses an Atharī interpretation; it does not claim a neutral consensus about the theology or authenticate its source-to-world application.

**Proposition 5C (finite conditional rival filtering).** Let $`R`$ be a finite declared family of complete rival assignments for a finite predicate registry $`\Pi`$. Define

```math
D(r)=\{q\in\Pi:r\text{ assigns }q\text{ false}\}.
```

For a selected set $`S\subseteq\Pi`$, every rival conflicts with at least one asserted member of $`S`$ if and only if $`S\cap D(r)\ne\varnothing`$ for every $`r\in R`$. Under warranted attribution, truth acceptance, referent identification, and applicability of those assertions, every such rival is excluded from the corresponding source-expanded class.

**Proof.** A rival conflicts with a selected assertion exactly when some selected predicate is false in that rival, which is exactly membership in $`S\cap D(r)`$. Universal quantification over the finite rival family gives the equivalence. When the selected assertions are warranted of the identified target, a rival falsifying one of them cannot satisfy the expanded premises. The conclusion depends on those premises; the intersection condition alone supplies no warrant for asserting them. $`\square`$

The predicates in this statement have truth values in the candidate assignments. Mere silence about a predicate or absence of evidence for it is not its falsity. An incompletely specified rival cannot be discarded just because the record omits a positive assertion. Nor does filtering an inconsistent source theory supply a credible winner: the expanded class must itself have an eligible interpretation. The practical comparison requires these semantic checks before applying the finite result.

One inherited example uses abstract order, impersonal origination, plural realization, a silent personal ground, a ground whose speech is only created, and a personal creator without the proposed revelational identification. Its minimum counts can be proved without inventing an objective explanatory score. In its exact assignment table, the created-speech-only rival lacks only the uncreated-Speech predicate; the plural rival lacks only oneness or the same-referent binding; and the last rival lacks only identification of the revealed referent with the neutral target.
