These three defect sets are pairwise disjoint. Every separator of the full six-rival portfolio therefore needs at least three selected commitments. The set consisting of uncreated Speech, oneness, and referent identification meets every defect set, so three is attained. [S24]

For the restricted first three rivals, knowing and oneness suffice. Knowing excludes the abstract and impersonal assignments; oneness excludes the plural assignment. No single registered predicate suffices, because the impersonal rival's defect set and the plural rival's defect set are disjoint. The minimum there is exactly two. These are results about the displayed predicate assignments, not neutral proofs of the attributed predicates or of the rivals' metaphysical possibility.

The full-portfolio count requires a qualification. Referent identification occurs in that counted commitment set, while it also serves as a standing condition of source application. Once identification is already fixed in the background, the rival that denies it has already been excluded. Among the remaining five rivals, uncreated Speech and oneness suffice; the disjoint created-speech-only and plural defect sets show that two additional predicates are still necessary. Thus the raw full-portfolio minimum of three and the residual minimum of two answer different questions. Authentication remains separately assumed in both calculations. Neither number counts all the work required to establish the source contract.

This clarification also prevents a source-critical error. Referent identification is an additional bridge, not a predicate established merely by the cited passage's using one name. The inherited reconstruction distinguishes true volition or speech attributes from unrestricted use of corresponding Divine Names, and distinguishes a necessary existent from a Creator conclusion. Its recorded access is to an English translation, not a fresh verification of the Arabic primary text. Those source qualifications remain part of its authority boundary. The mathematical filtering argument requires none of them to be silently strengthened. [S23–S24]

#### The content and level of the attributed source

For the speech commitment in this comparison, the public source note reports volition and speech as true attributes while withholding unrestricted Divine Name status from *al-murīd* and *al-mutakallim*. It also reports the Qur'an as uncreated Speech subsisting in and spoken by Allah. These are particular school-internal source assertions. Their co-predication with the other attributes of one referent has substantive force when the interpretation is accepted, as [Received Actuality and Intrinsic Necessity, §§10.11 and 10.16](received-actuality-and-intrinsic-necessity.md#1016-the-selected-source-argument-and-its-interpretive-conditions) explains. It is different from independently identifying that source referent with the target of a separate grounding argument. [S24]

The accompanying dossier also distinguishes several source arguments. Bare necessary existence does not yet identify the Creator of originated beings; a merely mental absolute does not supply the extramental existent sought by that argument; and the a-fortiori attribute rule presupposes an established Creator together with its praiseworthiness, absence-of-defect and nonassimilation qualifications. The commentary's reported rejection of a particular composition or indistinguishability proof does not withdraw its affirmation of unity or reject every possible unity argument. The separate current treatment supplies that criticism's restricted semantic content and preserves the stronger productive and necessity arguments. These attributions retain the dossier's translated-source status and reported locators; they do not constitute a fresh receipt or Arabic verification of the underlying text. [S23]

The source account of fiṭrah has a further evidential distinction. The dossier uses El-Tobgui's work as a secondary reconstruction of healthy, corruptible cognitive and moral capacities, and al-ʿUjayrī's as a contemporary Atharī account of innate recognition and its possible occlusion. The positive account of recognition is developed in [Recognition, Warrant and Restoration, §2](../epistemology/recognition-warrant-and-restoration.md#2-basic-recognition-and-the-possibility-of-rational-correction), with the additional function and application premises of §§5.3–5.4 above. Combining evaluation, disclosure, command, lordship, actualization and speech into one source-controlled architecture is itself a modern reconstruction. It has to preserve the argument and evidential level of each contributing claim; it is not reported as one theorem explicitly stated by a single historical source. [S23]

### 8.7 Why a source-dependent distinction does not become a neutral entailment

**Proposition 5D (premise-relative nonentailment).** Let $`\Gamma`$ be a set of premises in a declared semantics and $`C`$ a conclusion. If an admissible interpretation satisfies $`\Gamma`$ and falsifies $`C`$, then $`\Gamma\not\models C`$. A derivation of $`C`$ from a stronger set $`\Gamma\cup\Sigma`$ does not remove that countermodel to the original entailment.

**Proof.** Semantic entailment requires every admissible interpretation satisfying the premises to satisfy the conclusion. The stated interpretation violates that requirement for $`\Gamma`$. Additional premises may exclude it from the models of $`\Gamma\cup\Sigma`$; they do not change its satisfaction of the original premises. $`\square`$

A related language distinction explains the scope of this result. Suppose two expanded structures have the same reduct to a language $`L`$: the same domains, interpretations of its symbols, and any semantic relations required by its operators. Every $`L`$-sentence then has the same truth value in the two structures. For an ordinary first-order language this follows by induction: terms have the same values, atomic formulas have the same interpretations, Boolean operations preserve agreement, and quantifiers range over the same domains. Thus a difference confined to extra source predicates cannot itself be detected by an $`L`$-sentence. This pairwise observation does not assert that the entire expanded theory is a conservative extension; adding source premises can restrict which reducts remain admissible. [S23–S25]

There are two ways to exceed this boundary legitimately. One can independently warrant the additional premises or their sufficient equivalents at the intended comparison scope. Alternatively, one can give a different argument for the conclusion. The restriction concerns the dependence of this argument; it is not a demand that every possible argument establish every premise used by its predecessor. No permanent inability of reason, observation, or testimony follows.

The historical architecture comparison has this limited force. Its three candidates—a unified personal source, impersonal powers with plural realization, and abstract order with a concrete actualizer—agree on one selected neutral profile. That profile records underived order, formal cross-domain coupling, an additive revision field, finite articulability, token responsiveness, concrete realization, and availability of knowledge-like, selection-like, and efficacy roles. The target commitments concerning personal sourcehood and the richer attributed properties vary. Any target that varies within this attained profile fibre fails exact profile-only identification by Proposition 2. [S25]

This is not the assertion that the profile contains every neutral discriminator. An independently warranted coordinate that separates the conflicting pair would defeat the particular nonidentification witness. It is also not a claim that adding the desired properties to a candidate derives them from the profile. A formally described impersonal candidate can show insufficiency of the registered formal premises without thereby establishing its metaphysical possibility. The comparison is nonexhaustive, and a unique survivor in it need not be the only source-compatible architecture outside it.

The explanatory comparison requires an additional discipline. Counting bearers, grouping primitive features under one name, or listing unresolved premises does not yield an objective cross-kind cost metric. Shared derivation, restrictions across cases, and counterfactual dependence can be explanatory achievements; merely placing freely variable features in one bearer need not add them. The inherited comparison expressly supplies no warranted scalar aggregation, prior probability, or truth-conducive ordering. Its source-compatible survivor therefore cannot be promoted to a neutral explanatory winner just by counting its entries. [S23; S25]

The strongest compared impersonal alternative is more substantial than a collection of unrelated outputs. The comparison allows necessary concrete powers with genuine cross-domain laws, or necessary abstract modal, semantic, normative and articulability structure joined to a necessary or brute concrete impersonal actualizer. Its strongest composite combines these strengths with a primitive truth-linked norm, an applicability relation, and knowledge-like, selection-like and efficacy roles distributed among coordinated bearers. It therefore receives credit for the integration it actually supplies. Its applicability, coordination, norm and proposed teleology still require an account. Calling those commitments primitive neither refutes the proposal nor establishes that their explanatory cost equals the personal account's costs. The personal proposal aims to derive the relevant roles from the independently motivated nature of one necessary, externally actual personal ground; merely listing those roles under one subject does not accomplish that aim. [S23]

The retained variants challenge different premises. A coherentist or fixed-point account treats the total system as mutually constitutive and contests the demand for acyclic grounding. It cannot thereby count as a model of an acyclicity premise it rejects. A conceptual but nonpersonal field adds content-like structure while leaving the inference to a personal subject disputed. Grounding irrealism or brute totality contests the demand for a further ground; it is not automatically an alternative sufficient explanation satisfying that demand. Each must be assessed at the actual target and commitments it accepts. The affirmative arguments in §4 and in the foundation treatment remain part of that assessment. [S23]

The earlier comparison also kept source burden explicit: a dominance claim required no less coverage of the admitted explananda, no greater independently variable primitive burden, no stronger unverified source premises at the comparison's authority level, no additional surviving central countermodel, and at least one strict gain. Its finite registry implemented a declared diagnostic, not a canonical ordering of complete philosophical theories. A candidate can be preferred for satisfying an accepted source-predicate package without thereby winning that neutral explanatory comparison. Conversely, a historical failure to establish a bridge from a thin premise set does not settle an argument that independently warrants the stronger premises. [S23]

The earlier finite comparison used four rows with different declared neutral-coverage sets. The following table gives a token, or tokens, in each row's coverage that occurs in no other row's coverage. It also records coverage of the separate eight-predicate source target: Creator, unity, knowledge, power, will, life, speech and revelation. [S23]

| Earlier comparison row | Neutral-coverage tokens absent from every other row | Covered predicates in the eight-predicate source target |
|---|---|---|
| Unified personal source | Truth-link pressure; prior asymmetry | All eight |
| Necessary concrete powers with plural realization | Efficacy | Power and will: two |
| Abstract necessary order with an impersonal actualizer | Necessary order | None |
| Coherent fixed-point account | Holistic order; internal coherence; local articulability | None |

For any ordered pair of distinct rows, choose a listed token belonging only to the second row. The first row's coverage omits that token and is therefore not a superset of the second row's coverage. Thus the first required dominance condition fails in all twelve ordered comparisons; none can satisfy the full dominance rule. This establishes nondominance for those literal declared coverage sets. It does not prove that their differently named tokens are semantically independent, that the theories have equal costs, or that all their descriptions are realized. This earlier four-row result differs from the later three-candidate comparison with a shared selected profile treated below. [S23]

The separate source-predicate counts are eight, two, zero and zero. Conditional on accepting that exact source target, the personal-source row is the only complete compatibility match among these four entries. The target contains eight predicates; it is not the registry's separate eleven-item conditional-coverage field. This finite compatibility result neither authenticates its source nor supplies a neutral explanatory ranking or a proof of actual realization. It also leaves the current affirmative arguments for an original, necessary and personal ground to be assessed at their own richer premises. [S23]

The packaging result can be made exact. Let a plural explanatory profile have primitive space $`\Theta=\prod_{j=1}^n\Theta_j`$, explanandum space $`E`$, and output map $`F:\Theta\to E`$. Its declared primitive variations and represented dependencies are part of the comparison. Replace the primitive space by $`\widehat\Theta=\{g\}\times\Theta`$, and let $`p(g,\theta)=\theta`$. Assume that the new output map is $`\widehat F=F\circ p`$, that $`p`$ carries exactly the same admissible primitive variations in both directions, and that the new presentation adds no further cross-coordinate law, restriction or derivation. The carrier label is fixed; it does not restrict the values of the primitive coordinates. [S29]

**Proposition 5E (carrier-label invariance).** Under those hypotheses, the two presentations permit the same primitive profiles, determine the same explanandum profiles, and preserve every dependence expressed by their registered primitive variations. They are derivationally equivalent at this represented level.

**Proof.** The inverse of $`p`$ sends $`\theta`$ to $`(g,\theta)`$, so profiles correspond bijectively. The equation $`\widehat F(g,\theta)=F(\theta)`$ gives the same output at each corresponding profile. For any admissible variation $`i`$, its transported variation is $`\widehat i=p^{-1}\circ i\circ p`$ on the corresponding domain. Hence $`\widehat F\circ\widehat i=F\circ i\circ p`$. The same correspondence in the reverse direction preserves all registered variation/output comparisons and their dependencies. No extra restriction or derivation has been added, so the fixed carrier label supplies no further represented explanatory contrast. This is an isomorphism of the specified explanatory profiles, not a proof of equivalence between complete metaphysical theories. $`\square`$

If, in addition, $`E=\prod_jE_j`$ and $`F(\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_n)=(F_1(\theta_1),\ldots,F_n(\theta_n))`$, with independent coordinate variation still permitted, each output depends only on its own primitive coordinate. The account therefore remains a product of independent explanatory modules even when all features are attributed to one bearer. The argument would fail if a new law restricted the admissible joint profiles, if one primitive variation constrained a different domain, or if a shared derivation supplied a relation omitted by the original representation.

For example, two freely variable binary coordinates admit all four pairs. Giving them a common fixed carrier leaves all four pairs available. Requiring the coordinates to agree leaves only $`(0,0)`$ and $`(1,1)`$. This is a genuine new joint restriction despite retaining both marginal values; the one-coordinate flips of the unrestricted product are no longer always admissible. The example distinguishes a new relation from a new label. It does not establish the restriction's truth or the personal character of anything satisfying it.

The inherited application concerns modal identity, concrete actuality and efficacy, determinate content, and normative correctness. Assigning one bearer four freely recombinable primitive features for those domains does not yet derive their coordination. Shared derivation, restriction of joint possibilities, cross-domain counterfactual dependence, reduction of independently posited primitives, or exclusion of a previously permitted configuration could supply additional explanatory content. Rational agency is one proposed architecture for linking representation, evaluation, selection and efficacy; its links still require argument. The result neither denies ontological unity's possible value nor makes plural primitives preferable. It preserves the predecessor's exact limit: a reduction in bearer count alone is not a reduction in represented primitive explanatory freedom. [S29]

The distinction has antecedents in analyses of unification and parsimony. Kitcher relates unification to the reuse and stringency of argument patterns, including constraints against spurious unification. [P23] Kovacs's metaphysical unificationism likewise seeks to derive many explananda from few explanantia and argument patterns. [P24] Cowling distinguishes a theory's ontological commitments from its primitive ideological commitments; reducing the former does not by itself establish a reduction in the latter. [P25] McIntyre argues that exchanging ontology for ideology need not improve a theory and proposes explaining their costs through brute-fact parsimony. [P26] That proposal is a philosophical argument, not a general theorem equating every presentation's explanatory costs. Proposition 5E depends only on its stated profile map and transported variations, independently of further claims about grounding or essential existence. A defended dependence or intrinsic-necessity argument must be assessed at its own premises. These antecedents motivate the comparison; they do not establish this particular isomorphism or an exhaustive priority claim.

The architecture comparison also makes a narrower descriptive judgment. Write $`H_a`$ for an architecture's registered unresolved premises, $`K_a`$ for its admitted consequences, and $`B_a`$ for its unexplained stopping points. Its declared set-inclusion diagnostic requires no larger premise or stopping-point set, no smaller consequence set, no worsening of the registered surviving-countermodel condition, and at least one strict improvement. On the frozen three-row table no architecture meets these requirements against another. Already the premise-set test fails in every direction: each row contains a declared premise token absent from each other row. For the personal-source row one such token is the required common-bearer bridge; the impersonal-powers row has its powers-realizer premise; the abstract-order row has its necessary-abstract-order premise. Thus no row's full $`H_a`$ is a subset of either competitor's. [S25]

This verifies **no strict dominance in that declared diagnostic**. It does not prove that the premise tokens have equal costs, that differently named premises are semantically independent, or that every reasonable explanatory ordering leaves the alternatives incomparable. Identifying two tokens, discharging a premise, or changing the consequence and countermodel assignments would require a new justified comparison. In particular, the richer affirmative arguments elsewhere in these works must be assessed before transporting this historical table to their conclusions. The table is not an objective cost metric, and source compatibility still does not become neutral dominance.

The conclusion also runs in the other direction: the restricted comparison cannot erase a richer positive argument. Sections 4–6 defend additional intellectual, teleological, and normative premises, while §§9–11 develop actual source relations and truthful disclosure. [Received Actuality and Intrinsic Necessity, §§10.4 and 12.4](received-actuality-and-intrinsic-necessity.md) gives affirmative, defeasible arguments for actual connected-field source unity and informed practical agency. Those appraisals must be assessed at their own premises. A historical profile deliberately omitting their disputed explanatory relations cannot supply a global verdict against them. Their warrant does not, in turn, authorize treating every older bridge as an unconditional formal theorem.

#### A comparison reclassified as an authority reconciliation

The later three-architecture comparison has a documented sequence of scope repairs and a further correction to its theorem status. The [first cold audit][receipt-t-03] raised ten findings: the seven-coordinate registered profile was not all current or possible neutral evidence; source compatibility was not neutral explanatory dominance; the three candidates were not an exhaustive metaphysical partition; desired personal, Wisdom, Speech and revelation properties were commitments rather than neutral derivations; the premise/stopping-point diagnostic was not an objective cost metric; and a new eligible discriminator could reopen the comparison. It also required correct mathematical ancestry, source-authentication and referent-identification limits, proposal/adoption status and the distinction between procedural and external review. The [V2 repair log][receipt-t-05] retains each of these restrictions rather than treating the table as a permanent nonselection theorem.

The [V2 result][receipt-t-04] is `FAIL` despite reproducing the finite profile and authority tables. As the [blocking finding][receipt-t-06] explains, the frozen prose did not state two required scope clauses explicitly enough for its admission contract: the profile was not claimed to include every neutral discriminator, and source compatibility was not neutral explanatory dominance. The [V3 scope repair][receipt-t-10] added those statements without changing a mathematical object or substantive disposition. The [first V3 result][receipt-t-07] also failed, but for a different reason: the clauses were present across ordinary Markdown line wraps and the harness required single-line substrings. The [harness repair][receipt-t-01] normalized whitespace while preserving that failure. The later [V3 result][receipt-t-08] and [review report][receipt-t-09] record the resulting pass. The two failures must not be collapsed into either one mathematical defect or one successful execution.

The finite records describe one attained registered-profile fibre containing all three candidates and a conflicting personal/nonpersonal target. Only two Boolean classifiers exist on that one-element profile image; neither is exact, and the best matches two of the three stipulated rows. This fraction is a finite classification count, not an empirical accuracy estimate, probability of truth or justified prior over theories. A hypothetical independently warranted ownership coordinate gives two refined fibres without that target conflict. The four source-authority rows activate the conditional filter only when both source authentication and target/referent identification hold. Those truth-table values do not establish either premise in the world. The hash populations change with the reviewed edition: six frozen rows for V2, eight for V3 and twelve for V4, with no reported mismatch in those checks.

A successful semantic rereview still did not justify an independent theorem claim. The [theorem-family challenge][receipt-t-02] reopened the earlier Deep E comparison and found the source-nonmigration result, matched architecture comparison, conditional source leader and strongest impersonal rival already present. The [V4 reclassification][receipt-t-13] therefore treats profile nonselection as an application of the existing fibre family, source nonmigration as a corollary or restatement of Deep E, the architecture comparison as an updated application, and conditional filtering as a specialization using Deep S. The actual addition is the later evidence and premise-status reconciliation, an explicit reopening condition and its new review history. The identity names that reconciliation, not a newly originated theorem.

The [V4 review][receipt-t-12] and [result record][receipt-t-11] preserve the same scoped profile, countermodels and source filter while confirming that corrected ancestry. They do not override the distinct earlier four-row coverage comparison, the carrier-label isomorphism, or the later independently eligible-anchor results developed here. All are assessed under their own premises. The historical source guards remained required but unestablished at world authority, the source access was translated rather than newly Arabic-verified, the rival class remained nonexhaustive and the explanatory cost metric unprovided. No owner adoption, external human or independent model-lineage review, overarching programme closure or scientific priority follows. Nor does a restricted historical table defeat a richer argument whose additional premises are independently warranted.


### 8.8 Transport between evidence contexts

An anchor can legitimately move between contexts only when its intended use survives the move. This requires identification of the originating and receiving contexts, the anchor itself, its type, value semantics, version, authority, and applicability. Some cases preserve these directly; others need fresh justification. Extensional similarity of two finite partitions establishes an informational comparison, not the truth or entitlement of either input. [S26]

A positive sufficient case is transparent. Suppose an eligible anchor $`a`$ is replaced by $`a'=t\circ a`$, where $`t`$ is injective on its attained values. Then $`a(m)=a(m')`$ if and only if $`a'(m)=a'(m')`$, so the two anchors cover exactly the same target-conflict pairs. Replacing one by the other in a family preserves coverage and cardinality. The calculation establishes the information-preservation part of transport. It does not authenticate the recoding or warrant the receiving use. Those conditions must accompany the equality of maps.

The version example has two candidates: the same received bytes apply to an earlier recipient version and are stale for a later version. The required actions are respectively apply and hold. Receipt bytes are equal in both candidates, so receipt alone leaves the conflict uncovered. A properly interpreted version-compatibility coordinate with values true and false identifies the required action, giving minimum one in the version-aware registry. A separate authority coordinate, though required by the application contract, is true in both candidates and covers no conflict. Its failure to reduce the cardinality does not make it dispensable to warranted action. Capability, relevant invalidators, adoption, and actual execution likewise have their own roles beyond the profile calculation. [S26]

The source-world example has a different target. Two candidate interpretations share the same translated role profile but differ between personal and impersonal classification. Role profile alone gives an infinite minimum. The frozen table gives intensional predicate preservation the values true and false, and gives actual-world selection the values actual and nonactual. Either coordinate would separate the pair if independently eligible, so each is a one-anchor cover. The two coordinates nevertheless have different types and warrants. Preserving a predicate's intended strength is not selecting which candidate is actual; formally writing an actual-world selector is not possessing evidence for its value. [S26]

The stronger source package used in the architecture table makes the obligations explicit. Its source-to-world conditions include acceptance of the source regime; exact custody and source version; adequate translation; identification of the source bearer; an admissible interpretation family; unique candidate denotation under that family; actual-world selection; preservation of intensional content and predicate strength; identity with the proposed target; adequacy to the world; and applicability to this target. Its practical-role conditions add objective fittingness, apprehension of the relevant end or reasons, intentional uptake, selection because of fittingness, reliable realization, and co-instantiation of the required roles by the intended bearer. [S28]

These nineteen conditions constitute a declared sufficient contract. Their being conjoined makes every one necessary for satisfying that particular conjunction; a truth-table check of single-condition deletions does not independently prove that each is philosophically necessary for every possible source attribution or wise act. In particular, reliable realization makes this package stronger than the wise direction of §6. A fitting plan can be wisely chosen and fail in execution. The package must not turn that existing distinction into a contradiction by redefining all wisdom as successful performance.

#### What the source conditions rule out

The guard list acquires philosophical content only when its different failure modes are explained. The following interpretations exhibit specific missing implications. They are scoped formal examples and source-relative distinctions, not proofs that every imaginable combination of theological predicates is metaphysically realizable, or that the nineteen conditions are logically independent. [S28]

**Functional realization, uptake, and the reason for selecting.** In the six-bit construction the impersonal interpretation has exactly the same transition response to content and fittingness signals as the personal interpretation, while its additional uptake and because-fitting predicates are false. For example, changing $`c`$ changes the decision bit at fixed $`f`$, yet the transition formula never invokes apprehension or intentional uptake. Content sensitivity therefore does not entail those extra predicates within this formal vocabulary. The same interpretation allows $`f=1`$ while the predicate of objective fittingness is false. Registering a favorable signal cannot authenticate its own normative meaning.

A further distinction remains even if agency and a fitting outcome are granted. Keep the same agent, output history, and objectively fitting outcome, but interpret the additional relation “selected this act because of that fittingness” as false. This is a formal behavioral twin with different reason-basing, not a claim that reasons are metaphysically independent of every possible behavioral fact. No premise consisting only of the retained outputs and agency label entails the additional relation in that declared semantics. The positive account in §6 explains why an act's fittingness, its agent's uptake, and the reason for choosing it each matter. The stronger source package also requires reliable realization, while wise direction itself can survive an unsuccessful execution.

**Available roles and the bearer that has them.** A distributed interpretation can make every functional role available somewhere while assigning knowledge, selection, and efficacy to different bearers. The coordinating output then leaves open whether one bearer instantiates the entire required bundle. This is the common-witness issue treated by the incidence argument in [Received Actuality and Intrinsic Necessity, §10.10](received-actuality-and-intrinsic-necessity.md#1010-what-isand-is-notunified-by-the-construction); the kernel does not supply a new proof of that subject. Both the impersonal and distributed examples preserve the weak functional profile. They do not thereby satisfy a source interpretation that independently requires the very intensional predicates or common bearer they lack.

**Denotation, actuality, and application.** Let one source expression $`s`$ have two interpretations $`d_1,d_2`$ in the admitted family, with $`d_1(s)=u`$ and $`d_2(s)=i`$, while the two candidates have the same retained functional profile. That profile does not choose between the denotations. Conversely, fixing a unique denotation within each candidate world does not select which candidate world is actual. A set containing a source-compatible candidate gives an existential compatibility claim; selecting that candidate as actual requires another premise.

Application can fail even after a predicate is true of its referent. In a two-object interpretation take $`b\ne t`$, $`P(b)`$ true, and $`P(t)`$ false. A correctly attributed claim about $`b`$ does not apply to target $`t`$ without the identification bridge. If the predicate concerns an act, occasion, or recipient, its relevant application conditions also need to match; identity of an object alone does not settle every context-sensitive use. Finally, interpretability in a formal structure establishes satisfaction there. It does not prove that the structure adequately represents the actual world. These are different questions even where a particular theory supplies connecting premises.

**Causal production and the locus of an attribute.** The inherited source reconstruction uses an attribute-locus principle for speech. Its distinction can be represented by two objects $`b\ne q`$: $`b`$ causes an utterance to occur in $`q`$, while the speech attribute subsists in $`q`$ and not in $`b`$. Under that source-relative predication rule, $`q`$ is the speaking locus; the causal premise alone does not make $`b`$ that locus. The counterinterpretation separates causation from the rule for predicating an intrinsic attribute. It neither establishes the rule as a neutral metaphysical axiom nor decides that causing speech can never be relevant evidence for a richer attribution.

The source adjudication itself distinguishes three kinds of support. Its account of the Asfahani commentary reports access to an English translation: a predication of one Creator with selected attributes, a distinction between Names and attributes, a distinction between praiseworthy and blameworthy speech or volition, and the attribute-locus argument. That translated-source account is the support used here; it is not a fresh verification of Arabic primary text, authorization of an unrestricted Divine Name, or independent selection of an actual referent. The same adjudication classifies El-Tobgui's work as a secondary reconstruction of the relevant language, ontology, and epistemology, and al-Ujayri's as a contemporary Atharī argument from mastery, order, purpose, and repeated construction toward a knowing and wise agent. The latter identifies a substantive argument and its premises; recording it does not establish those premises by definition. These source classes are preserved rather than promoted to interchangeable formal proofs. [S28]

The positive source-relative result remains available. When the source is warranted at its appropriate evidential level, its intended predicates are preserved, the referent and target application are established, and the full practical-role premises hold, the declared strong wise-agency classification follows. The weak functional alternatives then fail the strengthened comparison wherever they deny its warranted predicates. A neutral experiment's inability to supply those premises neither supplies them nor defeats a richer argument that has established them. The purpose of the separate conditions is to make that positive argument assessable at the points on which it depends.

Architecture classification, recipient applicability, and source-world predication thus share one finite conflict-cover structure while retaining different substantive targets. No theorem of the common structure makes a version certificate evidence for personal agency, or turns a theological predicate into a common intervention. The useful transfer is the separation criterion with its assumptions intact.

### 8.9 Scope of the resulting comparison

The new formal layer identifies the exact remaining question for a fixed comparison: which independently eligible anchors cover its remaining target conflicts? It allows additional evidence to improve an inquiry, recognizes a source argument's conditional force, and makes illegitimate changes of meaning visible. It does not choose the true target, establish the actual model class, or manufacture a source's authority from its own classification.

The results assume finite declared candidates, stable total maps, and exact complete anchor values. No noisy or statistical identification theorem, unknown-class guarantee, adaptive acquisition bound, or actual-world selection follows. Existing §8.1 supplies an adaptive result under its different explicit conditions. The logical nonentailment argument likewise requires an admissible counterinterpretation, not merely a contradictory list labeled a rival.

The mathematical proofs and finite cases above stand independently of historical checking receipts. The corrected refinement condition is essential; historical finite trials cannot replace the general proof or authenticate the philosophical input. These results extend the existing account of cognition by locating precisely where warranted source content, new observation, or a substantive bridge can change a conclusion. They leave the positive arguments and their genuine unresolved premises available for direct assessment.

### 8.10 Structural covariance, content recoding and semantic identification

A structural rule can select an interpretation consistently across renamed copies without establishing what its contents refer to. Conversely, warranted reference information can distinguish interpretations that have the same structural pattern. The distinction is useful only when the relevant transformations, candidate interpretations and evidence are specified. The following finite treatment joins the public studies of natural interpretation and content recoding to the identification results of §§5.1 and 8.4. It retains their positive restrictions while repairing an ambiguous signature example and distinguishing removal of recoding ambiguity from identification by observation. [S30–S31]

#### Natural selection across structural copies

Let $`S=(X,\mathcal R)`$ be a finite relational structure and $`C`$ a finite content set. For every labelled copy $`S'`$ isomorphic to $`S`$, let $`I(S')`$ be a nonempty set of maps from its carrier to $`C`$. These are the *admissible interpretations*. If $`f:S'\to S''`$ is an isomorphism, transport an interpretation by

```math
(f_*\mu)(f(x))=\mu(x).
```

Assume transport carries $`I(S')`$ into $`I(S'')`$. Applying the assumption to the inverse isomorphism makes this a bijection. There is no action on $`C`$ in this structural transport: the content labels remain fixed. A natural selector assigns $`\sigma_{S'}\in I(S')`$ to every copy and satisfies $`f_*\sigma_{S'}=\sigma_{S''}`$ for every isomorphism. This is covariance across presentations, including invariance under automorphisms of any one presentation.

Write $`G=\mathrm{Aut}(S)`$ and

```math
J=\mathrm{Fix}_G I(S)
 =\{\mu\in I(S):g_*\mu=\mu\text{ for every }g\in G\}.
```

**Proposition (natural selectors and fixed interpretations).** Evaluation at $`S`$ gives a bijection between natural selectors on this one isomorphism class and the set $`J`$. Thus a selector exists exactly when $`J`$ is nonempty, and is unique exactly when $`J`$ has one member.

**Proof.** Naturality at each $`g:S\to S`$ makes the selected interpretation fixed. Conversely, given $`\mu\in J`$ and an isomorphism $`f:S\to S'`$, put $`\sigma_{S'}=f_*\mu`$. If $`h:S\to S'`$ is another choice, $`h^{-1}f`$ belongs to $`G`$ and fixes $`\mu`$; hence $`f_*\mu=h_*\mu`$. This defines the family independently of choices. For $`k:S'\to S''`$, transport composition gives $`k_*\sigma_{S'}=(kf)_*\mu=\sigma_{S''}`$. Finally any natural selector with value $`\mu`$ at $`S`$ must take exactly these values at all copies. The constructions are inverse. ∎

Equivalently, a fixed interpretation is constant on each orbit of the structural action on $`X`$. If $`y=g(x)`$, invariance gives $`\mu(y)=\mu(x)`$; if $`\mu`$ is constant on every orbit, the same equality proves invariance. The constraints defining $`I(S)`$ may rule out all such assignments or admit several. Two indistinguishable positions required to receive different contents give no fixed interpretation. If both constant interpretations into $`\{0,1\}`$ are admissible, they give two natural selectors. A rigid structure, whose automorphism group is trivial, has $`J=I(S)`$ and therefore supplies uniqueness only when the admissible family itself is a singleton. These are literal statements at the declared content-label level. A quotient of content assignments would pose a separately specified question.

#### Structural marks and the direction of monotonicity

Adding independently motivated structural relations or distinguished parameters reduces the automorphism group: for an expansion on the same carrier, $`H=\mathrm{Aut}(S^+)\subseteq G`$. If the admissible set $`I`$ is held fixed, then

```math
\mathrm{Fix}_G I\subseteq\mathrm{Fix}_H I.
```

Every interpretation invariant under all of $`G`$ is invariant under its subgroup. Thus adding structural marks can *increase* the number of natural interpretations. It does not, by itself, monotonically improve uniqueness. Conversely, removing marks cannot turn a single fixed interpretation into several fixed interpretations while the same admissible family is retained. The earlier example suggesting that direction needs either a changed admissible family or a different meaning of semantic multiplicity. [S30]

A three-position example gives the full behavior. Begin with three structurally indistinguishable positions and let $`I`$ consist of the three binary assignments having exactly one value equal to one. The full permutation group fixes none. Mark one position: the remaining automorphism exchanges only the other two, and exactly the assignment placing one at the marked position is fixed. Mark all three positions separately: the automorphism group becomes trivial and all three assignments are fixed. Existence can therefore change from none to one, and uniqueness can subsequently be lost. Every family in this example is stable under its declared group.

Changing the admissibility conditions is a different operation. A warranted rule that the marked position must receive a particular content can restrict $`I`$ and genuinely select a unique assignment. Its content and justification do the additional work. This preserves the source's positive point that explanatory resources matter, while locating exactly which resource produces the restriction.

#### Content transformations and the evidence map

Now equip $`C`$ with a declared finite algebra of relations, operations and constants, $`\Lambda`$. Let $`B`$ be independently warranted additional referential or semantic anchors expressed in that algebra. Its residual content group is

```math
\Gamma_B=\mathrm{Aut}(C,\Lambda\cup B).
```

These transformations act on interpretations by $`\mu\mapsto\gamma\circ\mu`$. Assume the comparison domain $`M\subseteq C^X`$ is stable under this action. Otherwise a proposed recoding may leave the admitted class and cannot be used as a same-class nonidentification witness without further argument. A content transformation preserves the admitted content structure; an arbitrary permutation that changes a designated truth constant or a relevant similarity relation is not necessarily admissible.

Let $`E:M\to Z`$ be the complete eligible evidence map of one fixed comparison, and $`T:M\to D`$ its target. A common experiment, its intervention meanings and its output coding must be held fixed across candidates. Exact identification means $`T=d\circ E`$ for a function on the attained evidence values. It holds exactly when

```math
E(\mu)=E(\nu)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad T(\mu)=T(\nu)
\qquad(\mu,\nu\in M),
```

by the fibre proof already given in Proposition 2. This condition also covers targets coarser than literal interpretation, such as one reference value or a truth classification.

**Proposition (recoding obstruction and its positive converse).** Suppose $`E(\gamma\circ\mu)=E(\mu)`$ for every admitted $`\gamma,\mu`$. If $`T`$ changes somewhere on a residual content orbit, it is not identifiable from $`E`$. If, in addition, the evidence fibres are exactly those orbits, then $`T`$ is identifiable if and only if it is constant on every residual content orbit.

**Proof.** Each orbit lies in an evidence fibre by invariance. Two orbit members with different targets therefore contradict the fibre criterion. Under the additional equality of fibres and orbits, an orbit-constant target is fibre-constant. Define the decoder at each attained evidence value to be the target of any member of its fibre; orbit constancy makes the definition well-defined. The reverse implication follows from the first part. ∎

The added hypothesis in the converse is substantive: invariant evidence can merge several different orbits. Orbit invariance is therefore a necessary condition for identification under invariant evidence, not generally a sufficient one. This theorem is the existing fibre-factorization argument applied to a group action. It does not show that the actual world has indeterminate meaning.

The same criterion gives a precise connection with evidence dissemination. If a copied or transformed record has the form $`H=h\circ E`$, every pair with equal old evidence still has equal forwarded records. A target-conflict pair unresolved by $`E`$ therefore remains unresolved by $`H`$, however many copies are made. If the full old evidence can be recovered by $`E=k\circ H`$, identification from the two records is equivalent, by composing the decoders in either direction. This is a deterministic information consequence, not an assertion that lineage roots or linear rank alone characterize semantics. The separate [copying and synchronisation account](../mathematics/collective-inquiry-and-strategic-coordination.md#14-copying-synchronisation-and-the-acquisition-of-evidence) specifies when roots and visible directions are retained; an additional measurement may enlarge the evidence beyond any function of the old record.

A literal interpretation is free of residual recoding ambiguity at $`\mu`$ exactly when

```math
\Gamma_B\mu=\{\mu\}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\gamma(c)=c\text{ for every }c\in\mu(X)
\text{ and every }\gamma\in\Gamma_B.
```

Indeed, equality of the functions $`\gamma\circ\mu`$ and $`\mu`$ is precisely that pointwise condition. The residual group can still act nontrivially outside the image of $`\mu`$. By contrast, identifying the literal interpretation *from the observed evidence at this case* requires $`E^{-1}(E(\mu))=\{\mu\}`$. A singleton orbit need not make that fibre singleton.

For an explicit counterexample, let $`X`$ have one position, let $`C=\{0,1\}`$ with both values designated, and admit the two possible interpretations. The residual content group is trivial, so both orbits are singletons. Let $`E`$ be constant. Their literal interpretations differ but their evidence does not, so no decoder identifies which is present. Removing a formal recoding symmetry has not supplied a measurement.

#### What the two symmetry layers imply together

The structural and content actions commute:

```math
g_*(\gamma\circ\mu)=\gamma\circ(g_*\mu).
```

Both sides evaluated at $`x`$ equal $`\gamma(\mu(g^{-1}x))`$. Suppose now that $`I(S)`$ is stable under both actions. Then its set $`J=\mathrm{Fix}_G I(S)`$ is stable under $`\Gamma_B`$: if $`g_*\mu=\mu`$, commutation gives $`g_*(\gamma\circ\mu)=\gamma\circ\mu`$.

**Corollary (unique natural selection constrains content recoding).** Under joint stability, if $`J=\{\mu_*\}`$, every admitted content transformation fixes $`\mu_*`$.

**Proof.** For every $`\gamma\in\Gamma_B`$, stability and commutation put $`\gamma\circ\mu_*`$ in $`J`$. Uniqueness makes it $`\mu_*`$. ∎

This is a composed consequence of the two source results. It is stronger than treating the symmetry layers as wholly unrelated. Structural *rigidity* alone only makes $`J=I`$; structural *existence* only makes $`J`$ nonempty. Either can coexist with content ambiguity. But unique natural selection and unresolved content recoding cannot coexist under the joint stability hypotheses. If a purported unique selector chooses one content name while a declared content automorphism sends it to another admissible fixed interpretation, the uniqueness claim fails. If the second interpretation is excluded, that exclusion must appear in the independently justified admissible family or content structure.

The corollary still does not identify arbitrary members of $`I`$ from arbitrary observations. For a concrete example let the structural group exchange two positions, let $`C=\{0,1,2\}`$, and let the content group exchange one and two while fixing zero. Take $`I=\{00,01,10,02,20\}`$, where the two digits give the contents at the two positions. The family is stable under both actions and its sole structurally fixed member is $`00`$, which the content group fixes. Constant evidence nevertheless leaves all five interpretations indistinguishable. If independently justified premises instead restrict the actual comparison domain to the singleton $`J=\{00\}`$, then every target on that domain is formally identified even by constant evidence. This is a real positive conclusion; the warrant for the restriction and its application to the case are doing the work. It does not authenticate those premises or identify an external referent solely from a formal name.

#### When an additional anchor really helps

Two monotonicities must be distinguished. Expanding $`B`$ to $`B'`$ gives $`\Gamma_{B'}\subseteq\Gamma_B`$ and hence smaller or equal residual orbits. Expanding the observed profile from $`E`$ to $`E'=(E,a)`$ gives smaller or equal evidence fibres. The first removes declared recoding symmetries; the second can resolve observations that the old experiment merged. An anchor may do both, but neither role follows just from naming it an anchor. The interpretation, eligibility and value of $`a`$ must remain fixed in the comparison, as in §§8.4–8.5.

For a positive two-case example, use a one-position structure and equality-only contents $`\{c_0,c_1\}`$. Admit both interpretations and observe only their equality kernel, which is the one-entry matrix $`[1]`$ in both cases. The content swap is allowed and changes literal reference. An independently warranted designation of $`c_0`$ removes that swap. If evidence remains the constant kernel, the two interpretations still cannot be distinguished. If a warranted common test also supplies $`a(\mu)=1`$ exactly when $`\mu(x)=c_0`$, then $`(E,a)`$ has two singleton fibres and identifies the literal value. The positive result needs both the correct meaning of the test and access to its actual outcome.

A source argument can instead establish a substantive restriction on the admissible class. If warranted source content identifies the intended referent and excludes an assignment, it changes the comparison as described in §8.6. The restriction can genuinely remove a rival. A preferred label introduced solely because it yields the desired conclusion supplies no independent warrant for that change. Likewise, naming a graph node “source,” retaining a source-shaped causal graph, or translating a role expression does not by itself establish which external entity it denotes. A warranted source-to-world map can supply that bridge; its existence is not ruled out by the group calculation.

Interventions have the same discipline. If every intervention label and response code is transformed along with the contents, the resulting descriptions may remain the same experiment under recoding. A genuinely enlarged common experiment can break the old invariance when its actual responses distinguish formerly equivalent candidates. Giving different candidates different target definitions, label meanings or interventions does not establish this discrimination within one fixed experiment.

#### Semantic form, constitutive content and its bearer

The resulting distinctions support four separately assessable claims:

| Claim | Exact question |
|---|---|
| Natural interpretation | Does a structurally invariant admissible interpretation exist, and is it unique? |
| Content identification | Are the relevant residual recodings removed, and does eligible evidence isolate the required target? |
| Constitutive semanticity | Does the semantic content belong intrinsically to the proposed ground, rather than being assigned by an external law or interpreter? |
| Mental realization | Is that content realized in a mental or intellectual bearer, with any further required unity or agency? |

The first two are answered by the declared finite model only at the stated scope. The latter two need further philosophical or empirical premises. A formal structure with a single marked position, fully designated contents and a singleton admissible interpretation can be expanded by assigning its interpretation through an external impersonal rule and setting the additional intrinsic-content and mental-host predicates false. It satisfies the uniqueness and recoding conditions without those predicates. If an account independently establishes constitutive content, a further implication to a mental host still needs its own premise: adding an intrinsic-content predicate to a formal description does not supply that implication by itself. These are counterinterpretations to an entailment from the specified vocabulary, not demonstrations that every such model is metaphysically possible. [S30–S31]

Distributed and downstream realizations expose two additional gaps. Different subsystems can realize the restrictions of one interpretation to different parts of $`X`$, while no single bearer owns the whole interpretation. A joint assignment therefore does not identify a numerically single subject. Separately, a downstream mind can form notions representing an external impersonal order; the mental location of those notions does not make the represented order intrinsically mental. The natural-selector predecessor relates this second possibility to El-Tobgui's secondary reconstruction of Ibn Taymiyya's distinction between external particulars and mentally abstracted universals. That attribution is retained at the predecessor's secondary-source scope; it is not a new inspection of the medieval primary text or a source proof of an ultimate mind. [S30; see also §8.8]

Local grammatical convergence has the same limit: several systems can realize one transition or similarity pattern while their admitted literal-reference assignments still vary. The invariant pattern is a positive common constraint, but selecting one ultimate mental grammar requires additional warranted premises. The same applies to proper function, personal agency, wisdom, necessary being, creatorhood or revelational identification. Those conclusions are not theorems of finite equivariance alone; the affirmative arguments elsewhere in this work and in the foundations and epistemology treatments remain available at their own premises.

#### Historical finite checks and the correction boundary

The semantic-gauge study's earlier finite calculation reported PASS, while its cold audit required nine specification and interpretation repairs. The revised argument had to fix a declared eligible evidence map and its invariance, distinguish formal content recoding from actual-world reference, and keep structural and content transformations separately specified. A proposed intervention must be one common experiment with a fixed interpretation and output code; changing those codes with the candidate does not show discrimination. The repair also made the residual anchor group explicit, separated relative literal identification from intrinsic semanticity and mentality, and kept source-based anchors at their independently justified authority. These are restrictions on the conclusions licensed by the model, not failures that a numerical PASS could settle. [Cold-audit findings][receipt-ay-cold-audit] [V2 corrections][receipt-ay-repair]

The primary record exhaustively checks eight declared three-content undirected structures and 216 interpretations: 210 have nontrivial interpretation orbits and six have singleton orbits, with no recorded orbit-profile failures. Designating content zero reduces the group in six structures and leaves four rigid. The distinct same-session rereview uses 6,000 sampled four-content structures with directed binary relations, unary predicates and optional anchors. It reports 48,000 interpretations, 3,399 nontrivial residual orbits, 44,601 singleton orbits and 272 structures where anchors reduced the group, again with zero orbit-profile failures. Its six frozen-hash rows have no mismatches. The different populations and exhaustive-versus-sampled methods remain separate; neither is a new execution by this incorporation. [Primary finite observations][receipt-ay-primary] [Four-content observations][receipt-ay-rereview-data] [Rereview method][receipt-ay-rereview]

The recorded controls include content-label swaps, hidden or target-selected anchors, rigidity of one symmetry layer without rigidity of the other, an impersonal semantic law and a downstream interpreter. Their purpose is to locate which premise changes the conclusion. Rigidity of a structural group alone is weaker than uniqueness of an admissible natural interpretation, so these controls do not remove the joint-stability condition or the unique-selector corollary proved above. Likewise, the source's singleton-residual-orbit criterion removes the indicated formal recoding ambiguity relative to declared anchors; identification by observations still requires the evidence-fibre condition. The constant-evidence counterexample above preserves this further distinction. [Rereview and bridge limits][receipt-ay-rereview]

The admission record leaves constitutive semanticity and mental realization open. Evidence-relative nonidentifiability does not establish metaphysical semantic indeterminacy, conventionalism or source falsity; eliminating a recoding ambiguity does not establish an intrinsic mental ground, personality, Wisdom, divine Speech or revelational identification. Source truth and actual-world reference require their separate warrant. The contribution is recorded as an application of familiar invariance and fibre reasoning, with no general mathematical novelty or historical-identity credit; external review and owner adoption remain open or pending, and repository adoption is not authorized by that record. These are the source's bounded dispositions, not fresh independent verification or completion of the wider research objective. [Admission, nonclaims and authority][receipt-ay-admission]

#### Representation studies and the exact scope of their evidence

Huh and colleagues define representation alignment through similarity kernels and hypothesize convergence toward shared statistical structure. Their idealized analysis requires bijective observation projections, and their limitations include differing information across modalities, specialist systems, incomplete convergence and dependence on the alignment metric. [P27] These are substantive claims about representation learning, not by themselves a determination of literal reference. The mathematical application is exact only for transformations preserving the selected kernel: any content bijection preserves an equality kernel, and an orthogonal transformation preserves a real inner-product kernel because $`\langle Qu,Qv\rangle=\langle u,v\rangle`$. An arbitrary relabelling need not preserve every numerical similarity measure.

The hippocampal study by Sun and colleagues reports progressive decorrelation into task-state representations. Its comparison gives the clone-structured causal graph model a stronger fit than the tested endpoint-only alternatives because it also reproduced the observed learning trajectory. [P28] That is a positive model constraint at the reported experimental scope. The implication drawn here is conditional: any remaining semantic transformations preserving that entire registered evidence still cannot be distinguished by it. The symmetry theorem neither denies the empirical result nor establishes that every possible expanded experiment has the same invariance.

Neither study is rerun here. The first attribution uses the inspected preprint's definitions and limitation sections; the second retains the published study's abstract and model-comparison account at that bounded receiving scope. They supply no independent proof that the modeled content is constitutive of an ultimate ground or belongs to one intellectual subject. A proposed wider conclusion must identify the additional evidence or premise that removes the relevant alternative. An impersonal semantic law or a downstream interpreter survives a comparison that has not independently excluded it; a richer justified argument can change that comparison. The exact finite proofs make both the positive restriction and the remaining task explicit, without turning either into scientific adoption. [S30–S31]


#### Selector proofs, source checks and the limits of a successful review

The natural-selector argument has a correction history that helps specify what its evidence establishes. An early [cold audit][receipt-am-02] required nine repairs: an isomorphism-stable admissible family with a declared transport convention; the full groupoid of labelled copies rather than invariance at one object; the distinction between existence and uniqueness of a fixed interpretation; a declared semantic identity or quotient; and separate arguments for constitutive content and mental realization. The remaining findings concerned the limits of representation alignment, the hippocampal model comparison, the source-relative mental/external distinction, and the result's standard group-action ancestry. The [repair record][receipt-am-04] addresses those conditions. A unique interpretation relative to one admissible family can still be specified by an impersonal law; changing that family or the intended notion of semantic equivalence changes the question.

The [primary finite record][receipt-am-09] reports 6,208 interpretations, of which 1,296 were fixed and 4,912 had a moving-automorphism witness; its 416 invariant three-vertex families included 384 with a selector. It records no copy-equivalence or selector-criterion failures and 5,888 equal-kernel/reference-swap witnesses. These are historical execution declarations, not new runs. The subsequent [distinct implementation report][receipt-am-03] used NetworkX vertex orbits rather than the primary custom permutation method. It reports 64 four-vertex graphs and 5,184 three-content interpretations, the same 416-family criterion check, 20,000 sampled five-vertex cases and 4,992 kernel/reference-swap controls, with no mathematical mismatch. The smaller [public summary][receipt-am-05] retains only part of that successful mathematical population; it does not replace the fuller record of source verification or its earlier failures.

Those failures remain substantive evidence about the review's limits. The [first result][receipt-am-06] is `FAIL`: two of thirteen literal source checks failed even though eight frozen-file and three source hashes matched and the model checks passed. One probe asked for “latent state inference” in an abstract that used “hidden state inference”; another expected a contiguous sentence across a two-column PDF extraction. The [second result][receipt-am-07] remained `FAIL`, with one of fourteen source checks failing after whitespace normalization. The [repair log][receipt-am-01] explains why whitespace alone did not remove the column-interleaving problem. The [third result][receipt-am-08] records fifteen successful source checks after checking the independently present fragments, with the same mathematical counts and no hash mismatch. This sequence repairs source-text matching. It neither changes the theorem nor retrospectively converts the two failed records into successful custody checks.

The empirical and philosophical restrictions above remain controlling. Kernel alignment is relative to a selected metric and available modalities; a better trajectory fit in the reported hippocampal comparison is evidence about the tested models. Neither establishes unique literal reference, intrinsic notionality, an ultimate grammar or a mental ground. The secondary source's mental/external distinction permits a downstream-abstraction interpretation unless a further argument excludes it. The existing structural-mark monotonicity, joint-stability and evidence-fibre arguments retain their own exact hypotheses; these finite records do not certify every extension of them. The distinct implementation took place within the same programme and is not external human or model-lineage review. The abstract selector result receives no general mathematical novelty credit, and the records supply no scientific or metaphysical adoption decision. [Cold-audit limits][receipt-am-02]; [rereview scope][receipt-am-03].


<a id="r132s-objective-receipts"></a>

### 8.11 Causal guidance, fittingness and the reason for selecting

The preceding account distinguishes possession of a correct standard from its causal use and from its authority. The score-guidance and reason-mediation studies sharpen these distinctions without discarding a successful mechanism. In the declared deterministic Boolean structural causal model, let the action equation be $`A=f_A(\mathrm{Score})`$, with the remaining equations held fixed under intervention. The interventions setting Score to zero and one produce different actions exactly when $`f_A`$ is nonconstant. This is a genuine causal statement **within that model**. It neither makes the encoded score a norm nor establishes that the model correctly describes an actual system. [R359-AA]

A hidden-common-cause control explains the qualification. One model gives Score a causal influence on action; another lets a hidden variable determine both. Their natural observations of world, score and action can coincide. At the recorded control $`H=0`$, intervening to set $`\mathrm{Score}=1`$ produces action one in the causal model and zero in the common-cause model. Observational agreement consequently does not certify the proposed edge. Equally, fixing the entire declared observation-and-intervention reduct still leaves room, in a comparison class admitting them, for expansions with different norm-authority, semantic-anchor, intentional-uptake, ownership and personality predicates. Such a pair establishes nonentailment in that class. Its existence as a formal interpretation does not establish metaphysical possibility, equal prior probability or physical realizability.

The positive policy result should also be retained. Let $`H(c,\theta)\subseteq A`$ be a supplied admissibility relation for a context $`c`$, profile $`\theta`$ and finite action set. A profile-aware policy selecting an element of every $`H(c,\theta)`$ exists exactly when every required fibre is nonempty. A profile-blind policy exists exactly when $`\bigcap_\theta H(c,\theta)`$ is nonempty for every context. The forward implications follow because the selected action belongs to the relevant sets; the reverse implications follow by choosing an element from each nonempty finite set or intersection. Distinct singleton fibres force every conforming policy to change its action between those profiles. Under the explicit equation $`\mathrm{Act}:=\pi(C,\Theta)`$, a surgical change of the exogenous profile input gives the corresponding limited intervention result. [R359-AB]

Neither existence criterion verifies what the registered relation means. Truth, target authority, world adequacy, source and version applicability, scope and history sufficiency remain distinct conditions. An arbitrary or malicious table can satisfy the selection theorem. Overlapping admissible sets need not force an action change, and a static registry says nothing by itself about an unregistered profile, drifting standard, hidden history or later policy revision. Knowledge, selection, execution and source custody can also be distributed among bearers. A successful policy therefore supplies neither common-bearer co-instantiation nor first-person ownership merely by its success.

There is a further operational gain when a genuinely irrelevant proxy can be separated from a registered reason variable. For a fixed policy $`\pi(c,r,p)`$, invariance under all permitted changes of the nuisance coordinate $`p`$ is equivalent to a factorization $`\pi(c,r,p)=g(c,r)`$. Choose any representative proxy value to define $`g`$; invariance makes the choice immaterial. Conversely a factorization has no remaining dependence on $`p`$. Conformity to the declared action relation, relevant reason contrasts and the appropriate intervention model can then establish **registered reason-variable mediation**. This is the familiar fibre criterion applied to a policy, rather than a new analysis of subjective motivation. [R359-AD]

The nuisance premise is substantive. A token can carry an authenticated source version, indexical content or extra evidence; erasing that information would not be a virtue of semantic invariance. Likewise, varying a reason label need not be an independently feasible intervention on content while the world, evidence and policy remain fixed. Hidden history can make the same current triple require different actions. Correlation, endpoint performance and similar representation trajectories cannot replace the missing intervention evidence. Even a correctly interpreted system-level mediation relation is weaker than a subject apprehending an act's fittingness and selecting it for that reason.

The crossed-design study gives a precise prospective experiment at this limited interface. Let $`F`$ be forms, $`C`$ declared contents and $`a(f,c)`$ the deterministic response under a feasible common intervention setting both. With at least two forms and contents and a nontrivial response alphabet, complete knowledge of the table decides whether responses are constant across forms at fixed content and vary across contents. Invariance is exactly factorization through the content coordinate; nonconstancy of the resulting content map supplies the additional sensitivity condition. Diagonal observations alone cannot decide it: a form-following and a content-following response can agree wherever the observed form and content happen to be paired. [R359-AZ]

For the unrestricted finite table class, omitting any cell prevents a universally correct classifier. Start with a nonconstant content-only table and change the response at just the omitted cell. Every received entry remains the same, while the changed table fails form invariance because another form at that content retains its former response. This necessity result concerns worst-case classification over that unrestricted class. A warranted parametric, compositional, monotone or source-constrained class can reduce the measurements needed; some particular partial tables can already establish failure. In the stochastic extension, the objects compared are complete interventional response distributions, not single sampled outputs. The finite identification statement supplies neither finite-sample accuracy nor the feasibility of the proposed interventions.

Semantic interpretation, positivity, consistency, absence of interference, fixed episode and history, source and version stability, and a candidate-independent coding and intervention design are all required for the intended application. Without the semantic premise the table concerns labels. With it, an impersonal semantic processor can still realize the full table. Intrinsic or constitutive content, intentional uptake, proper function, personality and wisdom remain further targets. This preserves the positive gain—an explicit test of form versus declared-content mediation—without presenting it as a test that already discriminates a personal ground from the strongest semantic impersonal rival.

The receipt sequence records the cost of obtaining these bounded statements. The first score-guidance audit raised seven scope defects; a later audit found the named earlier checker source absent. The subsequent edition introduced fresh post-boundary owners and explicitly did **not** reconstruct the missing historical file. Its recorded primary analysis covers 1,024 Boolean models, 512 with causal guidance; its procedural re-review reports 22,528 observation/intervention rows. The policy re-review checks all 1,728 admissible selectors in 343 small systems, alongside 50,000 larger blind-policy cases and 20,000 reduct comparisons; the primary report covers 117,649 systems. The reason-mediation reports separately record 256 primary policy tables and 20,736 policy/admissibility systems, then 60,000 larger tables with 29,998 factorable cases and 34 direct intervention rows. The crossed-design reports retain the 16 binary $`2\times2`$ tables, all 512 binary $`3\times3`$ tables, six content-mediated tables in the latter class and 30,000 stochastic-table trials. These are historical source-reported checks, with their differing domains intact, not new executions or a substitute for the arguments above. Their reviews describe distinct procedures within the same model lineage, not external independent review. [R359-AA; R359-AB; R359-AD; R359-AZ]

The mathematical ancestry is elementary finite selection, fibre factorization and factorial causal design. The philosophical source material has a different status: the policy study uses translated access to the Asfahani commentary, including its distinctions among praiseworthy and blameworthy volition and speech, without a fresh Arabic verification. Representation studies constrain the empirical models they actually compare; they do not authenticate a normative standard or establish the subject's ownership of a reason. A transparent bridge specifying uptake, fitting end, because-of selection and ownership can show what a proposed classification requires. It is not independent evidence for those very relations, and intentional purpose alone is weaker than the coordinated knowledge, fitting orientation and stable agency needed by the richer wisdom argument. The affirmative reasons in §§4–6 and 9–13 remain to be assessed at their own premises.

### 8.12 Asymmetry resources and the limits of a specification argument

The structural selection result in §8.10 also bears on the claim that a determinate outcome needs a specifier. Let a group $`G`$ act on a resource space $`E`$ and an alternative space $`A`$, and let $`f:E\to A`$ be an equivariant deterministic selector. Every element of the stabilizer of $`e`$ fixes $`f(e)`$, because $`g f(e)=f(ge)=f(e)`$. Consequently a resource state fixed by all of $`G`$ cannot select an alternative if the alternative action has no global fixed point. Transitivity on a non-singleton set is one sufficient condition for that absence; it is not the general hypothesis. [R359-AC]

The conclusion requires accounting for asymmetry, not identifying its bearer as a voluntary subject. A represented seed can supply a pointwise asymmetric resource. A constant selector can instead violate equivariance. A purported world application can fail correspondence, resource completeness or interpretation. The theorem distinguishes these possibilities only after its spaces and action are fixed. A stochastic kernel can preserve symmetry at the level of its law while producing particular outcomes, and an invariant multivalued relation can contain every alternative without selecting one. Neither is automatically the deterministic map just considered. Representing a realized seed changes the pointwise question; it does not prove that the probability law itself has selected a unique fixed output.

Nor does one output imply one explanatory source. Two resources may each suffice for it, or resources may be nested or jointly sufficient. Semantic apprehension of the alternatives, intention, selection because of a reason, ultimate grounding and common-bearer identity are not conclusions of stabilizer inclusion. Interpretations of the same parameter as brute, dispositional or intentional give a formal underdetermination control only in a class admitting those interpretations. Excluding every impersonal rival is a separate comparative task, not a premise whose inclusion would independently prove volition.

The source distinction matters here. The recorded specification argument first belongs to the translated creed; the commentary affirms selected predications while scrutinizing causal, regress and premise questions in particular specification arguments. Treating that sentence as an unqualified neutral theorem would erase both strata. The received study assigns the deterministic result to the existing T254 family and the world-use restrictions to T255, with no new general theorem-origin credit. Its primary enumeration reports 3,412 natural maps and 16,738 extended fixed-resource maps; its procedural re-review uses cyclic and dihedral actions, 77 cyclic shifts and 30,000 orbit constructions with 195,343 stabilizer inclusions. It retains multivalued, overdetermination and non-equivariant controls. None of those recorded checks verifies world correspondence, truth, warrant, complete resources or personal will. [R359-AC]

### 8.13 Exact determination and the interval of neutral bridges

Repeated additions of structural invariants cannot resolve a target that varies within one unchanged complete reduct. For a finite attained class, exact recovery is equivalent to constancy on the reduct fibres. If representation independence is intended, isomorphic reducts must also receive the same classification. Exact same-domain equality and isomorphism are distinct relations; a finite lookup on attained classes is not by itself a first-order definition over every model of a theory. Classical Beth definability supplies the latter implication only under its own ordinary first-order, all-models hypotheses. It is not established by the finite check and does not silently transfer to finite-model-only, modal, intensional, second-order or source-governed classes. [R359-AF]

The class and vocabulary remain load-bearing. Removing the impersonal expansion by definition, or adding a neutral coordinate defined to equal the personal target, produces formal determination by supplying the disputed answer. An independently warranted source restriction may instead legitimately change the admissible class. It then yields a source-relative result. The exact twin obstruction also leaves abductive support open: identical registered reducts need not have equal priors, equal explanatory merit or equal likelihood under additional evidence. The recorded finite study reports 759,375 five-fibre classes, with 1,024 exact-classifier cases; its distinct relational analysis reports 104 three-node isomorphism classes and 1,992 implicitly determined cases among 50,000 sampled intended classes.
